3.23
), we observe first that

\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}(\widetilde{E}-\widetilde{D})=C\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\,\sqrt{t}\,\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\frac{1}{t+\widetilde{D}^{2}}\widetilde{\Gamma}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\frac{1}{t+\widetilde{E}^{2}}

which implies that
\|\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}(\widetilde{E}-\widetilde{D})\|_{\text{op}}\leq C
. Then we write

\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}\widetilde{D}\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}}}\widetilde{E}^{1/2}-1=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dt}{t^{1/2}}\frac{1}{t+1}\,\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}\frac{1}{t+\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}\widetilde{D}\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}}\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}(\widetilde{E}-\widetilde{D})

to conclude that

\Big\|\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}\widetilde{D}\widetilde{E}^{-1/2}}}\widetilde{E}^{1/2}-1\Big\|_{\text{op}}\leq C

and therefore to obtain (
3.23
).
∎

As mentioned at the beginning of this section, below we have to evaluate the potential energy of suitable trial states. To this end, we make use of the following lemma that controls the conjugation of
\mathcal{V}_{N}
by the unitary map
\mathcal{U}
, defined in (
3.14
).

Lemma 3.4
.

There exists
C>0
such that in
\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}
we have

\mathcal{U}^{*}\,\mathcal{V}_{N}\,\mathcal{U}\leq CN^{-1/2}(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})+1)^{2}.

Proof.

Recall from (
3.14
) that
\mathcal{U}=e^{X}\mathcal{W}
. For
t\in[-1;1]
, we have

\begin{split}e^{-tX}\mathcal{V}_{N}e^{tX}=&\,\mathcal{V}_{N}+\int_{0}^{t}ds\,\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\alpha(x;y)\big(e^{-sX}a_{x}a_{y}e^{sX}+\text{h.c.}\big)\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}ds\,\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\big(e^{-sX}a^{*}_{y}a(\alpha_{x})a_{x}a_{y}e^{sX}+\text{h.c.}\big).\end{split}

(3.24)

With the operator inequality

v(x-y)\leq C\|v\|_{1}(-\Delta_{x}+1)^{\beta}(-\Delta_{y}+1)^{\beta}

(3.25)

for any
\beta>3/4
, we find

\begin{split}\Big|\int dxdy\,N^{2}&V(N(x-y))\alpha(x;y)\langle\psi,a_{x}a_{y}\psi\rangle\Big|\\
\leq\;&C\|\psi\|\|\mathcal{V}^{1/2}_{N}\psi\|\left[\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-y))|\alpha(x;y)|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\\
\leq\;&CN^{-1/2}\|(1-\Delta)^{\beta/2}\alpha(1-\Delta)^{\beta/2}\|_{\text{HS}}\|\psi\|\|\mathcal{V}^{1/2}_{N}\psi\|.\end{split}

Using Lemma
3.1
, part c) and then part e), to estimate
\|(1-\Delta)^{\beta/2}\alpha(1-\Delta)^{\beta/2}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq\|\widetilde{D}^{\beta/2}\alpha\widetilde{D}^{\beta/2}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C
, we obtain

\Big|\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\alpha(x;y)\langle\psi,a_{x}a_{y}\psi\rangle\Big|\leq C\langle\psi,\mathcal{V}_{N}\psi\rangle+CN^{-1}\|\psi\|^{2}.

(3.26)

Similarly, we can estimate

\begin{split}\Big|\int dxdy\,N^{2}&V(N(x-y))\langle\psi,a^{*}_{y}a(\alpha_{x})a_{x}a_{y}\psi\rangle\Big|\\
\leq\;&C\|\mathcal{V}^{1/2}_{N}\psi\|\left[\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-y))\|a^{*}(\alpha_{x})a_{y}\psi\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\\
\leq\;&CN^{-1/2}\|(1-\Delta)^{1/2}\alpha\|_{\text{HS}}\|\mathcal{V}^{1/2}_{N}\psi\|\|(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N})^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\psi\|\\
\leq\;&C\langle\psi,\mathcal{V}_{N}\psi\rangle+CN^{-1}\langle\psi,(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N})(\mathcal{N}+1)\psi\rangle.\end{split}

(3.27)

Inserting (
3.26
), (
3.27
) in (
3.24
), using Lemma
3.1
to replace
\mathcal{K}
with
d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})
and then applying (
3.21
), we arrive at

\begin{split}\langle\psi,e^{-tX}\mathcal{V}_{N}e^{tX}\psi\rangle\leq\;&C\int_{0}^{t}ds\langle e^{-sX}\psi,\mathcal{V}_{N}e^{sX}\psi\rangle+\langle\psi,\mathcal{V}_{N}\psi\rangle\\
&+CN^{-1/2}\langle\psi,(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\psi\rangle.\end{split}

With Gronwall, we obtain

\langle\psi,e^{-X}\mathcal{V}_{N}e^{X}\psi\rangle\leq C\langle\psi,\mathcal{V}_{N}\psi\rangle+CN^{-1/2}\langle\psi,(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\psi\rangle.

Since
\mathcal{U}=e^{X}\mathcal{W}
, we find, using (
3.22
),

\begin{split}\langle\psi,\mathcal{U}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{N}\mathcal{U}\psi\rangle&\leq C\langle\psi,\mathcal{W}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{N}\mathcal{W}\psi\rangle+CN^{-1/2}\langle\psi,\mathcal{W}^{*}(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\mathcal{W}\psi\rangle\\
&\leq C\langle\psi,\mathcal{W}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{N}\mathcal{W}\psi\rangle+CN^{-1/2}\langle\psi,(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\psi\rangle.\end{split}

(3.28)

To estimate the expectation of
\mathcal{W}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{N}\mathcal{W}
, we observe that, on the
n
-particle sector, using (
3.25
) and Lemma
3.1
,
\mathcal{W}=\Gamma(W)
and the bound
W^{*}\widetilde{E}W\leq C\widetilde{E}
established after (
3.22
),

\begin{split}\langle\psi^{(n)},\mathcal{W}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{N}\mathcal{W}\psi^{(n)}\rangle&=\sum_{i<j}^{n}\langle\mathcal{W}\psi^{(n)},N^{2}V(N(x_{i}-x_{j}))\mathcal{W}\psi^{(n)}\rangle\\
&\leq CN^{-1}\sum_{i<j}^{n}\langle\mathcal{W}\psi^{(n)},(1-\Delta_{x_{i}})(1-\Delta_{x_{j}})\mathcal{W}\psi^{(n)}\rangle\\
&\leq CN^{-1}\sum_{i<j}^{n}\langle\psi^{(n)},(W^{*}\widetilde{E}W)_{i}(W^{*}\widetilde{E}W)_{j}\psi^{(n)}\rangle\\
&\leq CN^{-1}\sum_{i<j}^{n}\langle\psi^{(n)},\widetilde{E}_{i}\widetilde{E}_{j}\psi^{(n)}\rangle\leq CN^{-1}\langle\psi^{(n)}d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})^{2}\psi^{(n)}\rangle.\end{split}

From (
3.28
), we obtain

\langle\psi,\mathcal{U}^{*}\mathcal{V}_{N}\mathcal{U}\psi\rangle\leq CN^{-1/2}\langle\psi,(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})+1)^{2}\psi\rangle.

∎

Equipped with Lemmas
3.3
and
3.4
, let us now bound the spectrum
\sigma(H_{N})
from above. Like for the lower bound, we make use of the min-max principle and we derive upper bounds on the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{J}_{N}
. We first control the ground state energy
E_{N}
and afterwards the spectrum of
\mathcal{J}_{N}
above
E_{N}
.

Recalling the definition of the unitary map
\mathcal{U}:\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\to\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}
in (
3.14
), the ground state energy of
\mathcal{J}_{N}
is bounded from above by

E_{N}=\lambda_{0}(\mathcal{J}_{N})\leq\frac{\langle\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega,\mathcal{J}_{N}\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega\rangle}{\langle\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega,\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega\rangle}.

Here,
\Omega=(1,0,0,\dots)\in\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}
denotes the vacuum and we abbreviate
\chi_{\leq N}=1(\mathcal{N}\leq N)
. Similarly, we write in the following
\chi_{>N}=1(\mathcal{N}>N)
so that
1=\chi_{\leq N}+\chi_{>N}
in
\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}
. By Markov’s inequality and (
3.18
), we note that

\|\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega\|^{2}=1-\|\chi_{>N}\mathcal{U}\Omega\|^{2}\geq 1-N^{-2}\|\mathcal{N}\mathcal{U}\Omega\|^{2}\geq 1-CN^{-2}.

Using (
2.46
), (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.4
), the fact that
\mathcal{J}_{N}\geq 0
and that
\mathcal{K},\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},\mathcal{V}_{N}\geq 0
commute with
\mathcal{N}
, we obtain

\begin{split}E_{N}&\leq(1-C/N^{2})^{-1}\big(\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\langle\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega,\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega\rangle\big)+C\,\langle\mathcal{U}\Omega,\mathcal{V}_{N}\mathcal{U}\Omega\rangle\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+CN^{-\rho}\langle\mathcal{U}\Omega,(\mathcal{H}_{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}\mathcal{U}\Omega\rangle.\end{split}

Applying Lemmas
3.3
and
3.4
, this implies

\begin{split}E_{N}&\leq(1-C/N^{2})^{-1}\big(\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\langle\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega,\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\Omega\rangle\big)+CN^{-\rho}.\end{split}

To evaluate this further, we use that in the sense of forms in
\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}
, we have

\pm\big(\chi_{\leq N}\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}\chi_{\leq N}-\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}\big)\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)

(3.29)

which is a straightforward consequence of the bound (
3.6
) and Markov’s inequality. Combining this with the identity (
3.15
), we find that

\begin{split}E_{N}&\leq(1-C/N^{2})^{-1}\left(\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2})-\widetilde{D}-\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)\right)+CN^{-\rho}\\
&\leq\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2})-\widetilde{D}-\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)+CN^{-\rho}.\end{split}

Note that in the last step we used
\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}-\widetilde{D}-\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)=\mathcal{O}(N)
, which is a consequence of the first inequality in the previous bound, the lower bound (
3.16
) and the fact that
E_{N}=\mathcal{O}(N)
. Thus, in summary, we obtain together with (
3.16
) that

E_{N}=\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\right)-\widetilde{D}-\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\rho}).

(3.30)

To continue with the higher min-max values of
\mathcal{J}_{N}
, we assume that
\lambda_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{N})
is such that
\lambda_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{N})-E_{N}\leq\zeta
. By (
3.16
) and the identity (
3.30
), we also have

\lambda_{n}\big(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})\big)\leq\zeta+CN^{-\rho}(1+\zeta^{6}).

(3.31)

Now, let us recall that

\lambda_{n}\big(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})\big)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}l_{j}^{(n)}\widetilde{e}_{j},

where the
(\widetilde{e}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
denote the eigenvalues of
\widetilde{E}
and the
l^{(n)}_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
are non-zero for finitely many
j\in\mathbb{N}
. As we did in (
3.14
), let us also denote by
(\varphi_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
an orthonormal eigenbasis of
\widetilde{E}
such that
\widetilde{E}\varphi_{j}=\widetilde{e}_{j}\varphi_{j}
, for
j\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, we choose
n
orthonormal eigenvectors
(\xi_{k})_{k=1}^{n}
of the form

\xi_{k}=C_{k}\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\big(a^{*}(\varphi_{j})\big)^{l^{(k)}_{j}}\Omega\in\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}},

with
C_{k}
ensuring that
\|\xi_{k}\|=1
for each
k=1,\dots,n
and such that

d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})\xi_{k}=\lambda_{k}\big(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})\big)\xi_{k}\hskip 14.22636pt\text{for }k=1,\dots,n.

Given the vectors
(\xi_{k})_{k=1}^{n}
, we define the linear space
Y^{(n)}\subset\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
by

Y^{(n)}=\chi_{\leq N}\,\mathcal{U}\,\text{span}(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}),

which we use below in the min-max principle to get an upper bound on
\lambda_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{N})
. Before evaluating the upper bound, note that
\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\,:\,\text{span}\big(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n})\to Y^{(n)}
is invertible, because we have for a normalized
\xi\in\mathcal{U}\,\text{span}(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n})
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
large enough (but fixed) that

\|\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\,\xi\|^{2}\geq\|\xi\|^{2}-\|\chi_{>N}\mathcal{U}\,\xi\|^{2}\geq 1-\big(\zeta+CN^{-\rho}(1+\zeta^{6})\big)^{m}N^{-m}>\frac{1}{2}

(3.32)

for
N
large enough. Indeed, the second bound follows from Markov’s inequality, the assumption
\zeta\leq CN^{-\rho/5-\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon>0
, the bound (
3.18
), the fact that
\mathcal{N}
commutes with
d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})
, the operator bound
\mathcal{N}\leq Cd\Gamma(\widetilde{E})
in
\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}
and because
\lambda_{k}\big(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})\big)\leq\zeta+CN^{-\rho}(1+\zeta^{6})
for each
k=1,\dots,n
by (
3.31
). Thus, by (
3.32
),
Y^{(n)}
is in particular
n
-dimensional.

Now, let us control
\lambda_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{N})
through the min-max principle. We obtain with (
2.46
), (
3.1
), (
3.3
), (
3.4
), (
3.29
), (
3.32
) as well as Lemmas
3.3
and
3.4

that for
m\in\mathbb{N}
large (but fixed) and
N
large enough

\begin{split}\lambda_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{N})&\leq\sup_{\xi\in Y^{(n)},\|\xi\|=1}\langle\xi,\mathcal{J}_{N}\xi\rangle=\sup_{\xi\in\,\text{span}(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}),\|\xi\|=1}\frac{\langle\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\xi,\mathcal{J}_{N}\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\xi\rangle}{\langle\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\xi,\chi_{\leq N}\mathcal{U}\xi\rangle}\\
&\leq\big(1-(N^{-1}\zeta+CN^{-1-\rho}(1+\zeta^{6}))^{m}\big)^{-1}\sup_{\xi\in\,\text{span}(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}),\|\xi\|=1}\big(\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\langle\mathcal{U}\xi,\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}\mathcal{U}\xi\rangle\big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+CN^{-\rho}\sup_{\xi\in\,\text{span}(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}),\|\xi\|=1}\langle\xi,(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)^{4}\xi\rangle\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+CN^{-1/2}\sup_{\xi\in\,\text{span}(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}),\|\xi\|=1}\langle\xi,(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})+1)^{2}\xi\rangle.\end{split}

(3.33)

Note that we used here the bounds
d\Gamma(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})\leq Cd\Gamma(\widetilde{E})
and
\mathcal{V}_{N}\leq\mathcal{K}\mathcal{N}
. To bound the right hand side in (
3.33
) further, we use that
\mathcal{N}
commutes with
d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})
,
\mathcal{N}\leq Cd\Gamma(\widetilde{E})
in
\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}
, the a priori estimate (
3.31
) as well as the identities (
3.15
) and (
3.30
) so that

\begin{split}\lambda_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{N})&\leq E_{N}+\lambda_{n}\big(d\Gamma(\widetilde{E})\big)+CN^{-\rho}\big(1+\zeta^{5}+N^{-5\rho}\zeta^{30}\big).\\
\end{split}

Finally, applying Lemma
3.2
and once again (
3.31
), we arrive at

\begin{split}\lambda_{n}(\mathcal{J}_{N})&\leq E_{N}+\lambda_{n}\big(d\Gamma(E)\big)+CN^{-\rho}\big(1+\zeta^{5}+N^{-5\rho}\zeta^{30}\big).\end{split}

(3.34)

3.3
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section, we conclude the proof of Theorem
1.1
, based on the upper and lower bounds on the spectrum
\sigma(H_{N})
from Sections
3.1
and
3.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Recalling (
3.30
), we have shown that

E_{N}=\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\right)-\widetilde{D}-\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\rho})

(3.35)

with
\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}},\,\widetilde{\Gamma}
from Proposition
2.8
and with
\widetilde{D},\widetilde{E}
defined in (
3.9
). Moreover, the bounds (
3.17
), (
3.34
) show that if
\lambda_{n}(H_{N})-E_{N}\leq\zeta
, then we have for sufficiently large
N
that

\lambda_{n}(H_{N})-E_{N}=\lambda_{n}\big(d\Gamma(E)\big)+\mathcal{O}\big(N^{-\rho}(1+\zeta^{5}+N^{-5\rho}\zeta^{30})\big),

with
E
defined in (
1.6
). The last identity proves the validity of (
1.10
) in Theorem
1.1
.

To finish the proof of the theorem, it now remains to verify the identity (
1.8
). This follows from evaluating the constant
\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}(\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}-\widetilde{D}-\widetilde{\Gamma})/2
on the r.h.s. of (
3.35
), up to errors that vanish in the limit
N\to\infty
. To this end, it will be convenient to abbreviate by
\widetilde{K}_{N}:L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\to L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
the operator with kernel

\widetilde{K}_{N}(x;y)=N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)

and to denote by
K_{N}:L^{2}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\to L^{2}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
the operator

K_{N}=Q\,\widetilde{K}_{N}\,Q,

where we recall that
Q=1-|\varphi_{0}\rangle\langle\varphi_{0}|
. Now, inserting the definitions of
\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}
,
\widetilde{D}
and
\widetilde{\Gamma}
into (
3.35
), we use cyclicity of the trace and
1=f_{\ell}+w_{\ell}
,
\cosh^{2}=1+\sinh^{2}
to write

\begin{split}E_{N}&=N\int dx\,\Big(|\nabla\varphi_{0}(x)|^{2}+V_{\text{ext}}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\big(N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}\big)(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{N}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)-4\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\int dx\,\varphi_{0}^{4}(x)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle|^{2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\operatorname{tr}\big(\gamma\big[N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big]\sigma\big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\right)-\widetilde{D}-\widetilde{\Gamma}\right)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\rho}).\end{split}

Applying Lemma
2.2
, we also find that

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle|^{2}\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt=-\frac{N}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw^{2}_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt-\operatorname{tr}\big(\gamma\big[N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big]\sigma\big)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}),\end{split}

which implies (recalling the definition (
2.11
) of the kernel
k
) that

\begin{split}E_{N}&=N\int dx\,\Big(|\nabla\varphi_{0}(x)|^{2}+V_{\text{ext}}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\big(N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}\big)(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-4\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\right)-H_{\text{GP}}-K_{N}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\big(\widetilde{K}_{N}k\big)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\rho}).\end{split}

(3.36)

To evaluate the right hand side in (
3.36
) we use Lemma
2.1

ii)
, switch to Fourier space and apply Plancherel’s theorem to find

\begin{split}&\int dx\,\big(N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}\big)(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)=\int dp\,\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(p/N)\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\\
&=\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(0)\int dx\,\varphi_{0}^{4}(x)-N^{-1}\int dx\,V(x)f_{\ell}(x)\,x\cdot\int dy\,\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\nabla(\varphi_{0}^{2})(y)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2})\\
&=\big(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}+12\pi\mathfrak{a}^{2}_{0}\,\ell^{-1}N^{-1}\big)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-2}).\end{split}

Note here that
\int dx\,V(x)f_{\ell}(x)x=0
by radial symmetry of
V
and
f_{\ell}
.

Next, using the scattering equation (
2.6
), we write for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}

-Nw_{\ell}(Nx)=\int\frac{dy}{4\pi}\,\frac{1}{|x-y|}\Big(-\frac{1}{2}N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(Ny)+N^{3}\lambda_{\ell}f_{\ell}(Ny)\chi_{\ell}(y)\Big)

and obtain with similar arguments as above that

\begin{split}-\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}\big(\widetilde{K}_{N}k\big)=&\,\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}((p-q)/N)\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(q/N)|q|^{-2}\\
&-\frac{N^{3}\lambda_{\ell}}{8\pi^{2}}\int dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}((p-q)/N)\widehat{\chi}_{\ell}(q)|q|^{-2}\\
&+\frac{\lambda_{\ell}}{8\pi^{2}}\int dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}((p-q)/N)\widehat{w_{\ell}}(q/N)|q|^{-2}\\
=&\,\frac{1}{16\pi^{2}}\int dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}((p-q)/N)\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(q/N)|q|^{-2}\\
&-\frac{N^{3}\lambda_{\ell}}{8\pi^{2}}\int dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}((p-q)/N)\widehat{\chi}_{\ell}(q)|q|^{-2}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}),\end{split}

where, in the last step, we used Lemma
2.1

i)
, the bound (
2.10
) and the inequality
\|\widehat{w_{\ell}}\|_{\infty}\leq\|w_{\ell}\|_{1}\leq CN^{2}\ell^{2}
. Since
|\widehat{\chi}_{\ell}(q)|\leq C|q|^{-2}
, Lemma
2.1

i)
,
ii)
and a first order Taylor expansion also imply

\begin{split}&\frac{N^{3}\lambda_{\ell}}{8\pi^{2}}\int dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}((p-q)/N)\widehat{\chi}_{\ell}(q)|q|^{-2}\\
&=\frac{N^{3}\lambda_{\ell}}{8\pi^{2}}\int_{|q|\leq N}dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}((p-q)/N)\widehat{\chi}_{\ell}(q)|q|^{-2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{N^{3}\lambda_{\ell}}{8\pi^{2}}\int_{|q|>N}dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}((p-q)/N)\widehat{\chi}_{\ell}(q)|q|^{-2}\\
&=3\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{2}\,\pi^{-2}\ell^{-3}\!\!\int dpdq\,\big|\widehat{(\varphi_{0}^{2})}(p)\big|^{2}\widehat{\chi}_{\ell}(q)|q|^{-2}+\mathcal{O}(\log N/N)=6\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}^{2}\,\ell^{-1}\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}+\mathcal{O}(\log N/N)\end{split}

so that

\begin{split}E_{N}&=N\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\varphi_{0})-4\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{4}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\bigg[\frac{1}{4\pi|.|}\ast N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N.)\bigg](x-y)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\right)-H_{\text{GP}}-K_{N}\right)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-\rho}).\end{split}

(3.37)

It remains to combine the last two lines in (
3.37
). We can rewrite

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{4}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\bigg[\frac{1}{4\pi|.|}\ast N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N.)\bigg](x-y)\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\\
&=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left[\widetilde{K}_{N}\varphi_{0}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\big]\varphi_{0}\right],\end{split}

where
\varphi_{0}
acts as multiplication operator in position space and where
\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)
acts as Fourier multiplier. Proceeding similarly as in
[
21
, Eq. (64)]
, we first rewrite

\begin{split}&\operatorname{tr}\left[\widetilde{K}_{N}\varphi_{0}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\big]\varphi_{0}\right]\\
&=\operatorname{tr}\left[\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{K}_{N}\varphi_{0}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\big]\varphi_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\varphi_{0}\big[\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)(-\Delta)^{-1}\big]\varphi_{0}\widetilde{K}_{N}\right]\\
&=\operatorname{tr}\left[\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{K}_{N}\varphi_{0}\big[(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\big]\varphi_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\varphi_{0}\big[\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}\big]\varphi_{0}\widetilde{K}_{N}\right]\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+\frac{\kappa^{2}}{2}\operatorname{tr}\left[\widetilde{K}_{N}\varphi_{0}\big[(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}(-\Delta)^{-1}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\big]\varphi_{0}+\text{h.c.}\right]\\
\end{split}

for a parameter
\kappa>0
that later will be chosen large enough. Plugging in
\widetilde{K}_{N}=\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}
, we have

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\left(\varphi_{0}^{2}\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}+\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}\varphi_{0}^{2}\right)\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\\
&=\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\left(\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}\right)\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\left(\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}[\varphi_{0},-\Delta]\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}[\varphi_{0},-\Delta]\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}\right)\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\left(\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}|\nabla\varphi_{0}|^{2}\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}\right)\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}.\end{split}

Inserting an additional projection
Q=1-|\varphi_{0}\rangle\langle\varphi_{0}|
, we arrive at

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left[\widetilde{K}_{N}\varphi_{0}\big[(-\Delta)^{-1}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\big]\varphi_{0}\right]=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left[K_{N}\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}}K_{N}\right]+\sum_{i=1}^{6}X_{i},\end{split}

(3.38)

where we defined

\begin{split}\text{X}_{1}&=\frac{\kappa^{2}}{8}\operatorname{tr}\left[\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}^{2}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}(-\Delta)^{-1}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}+\text{h.c.}\right],\\
\text{X}_{2}&=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left[\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\left(\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}[\varphi_{0},-\Delta]\right)^{2}\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\right],\\
\text{X}_{3}&=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left[\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\left(\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}|\nabla\varphi_{0}|^{2}\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}\right)\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\right],\\
\text{X}_{4}&=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left[\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\left(\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}-\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2}}\right)\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\right],\\
\text{X}_{5}&=\frac{1}{4\kappa^{2}}\|\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}^{2}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{-1/2}Q\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}^{2}\|^{2},\\
\text{X}_{6}&=-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left[K_{N}\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})}K_{N}\right].\end{split}

(3.39)

Observe here, under the assumption
V\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, we have
0\leq\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}(K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1}K_{N})=\|K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}Q\|_{\text{HS}}^{2}\leq CN<\infty
. Thus, the operator
K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1}K_{N}
is of trace class and the first term on the r.h.s. of (
3.38
) is well-defined.

Next, we show that the terms
\text{X}_{1}
to
\text{X}_{6}
, defined in (
3.39
), are
\mathcal{O}(1)
and that, in the limit
N\to\infty
, we can replace in each of them the Fourier multiplier
\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)
by
8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}
. We start with
\text{X}_{1}
. Since
\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)
and
\varphi_{0}
have bounded operator norm, we easily find (with Lemma
A.1
)

|\text{X}_{1}|\leq C\big\|\varphi_{0}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1/2}(-\Delta)^{-1/2}\big\|_{\text{HS}}^{2}\leq C.

Similarly, we can bound, for any
\alpha<1
,

\begin{split}\Big|\text{X}_{1}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{4}&(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0})^{2}\operatorname{tr}\left[\varphi_{0}^{2}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}(-\Delta)^{-1}\varphi_{0}^{2}\right]\Big|\\
\leq\;&C\|[\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}]\varphi_{0}^{2}(-\Delta)^{-1/2}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-3/4+\varepsilon}\|_{\text{HS}}\\
&+C\|(-\Delta)^{-1/2}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-3/4+\varepsilon}[\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}]\varphi_{0}^{2}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq CN^{-\alpha}\end{split}

(3.40)

using that
|\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(p/N)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}|\leq C(|p|/N)^{\alpha}
(from the compact support of
V
),
\|\Delta\varphi_{0}^{2}\|\leq C
(from Lemma
A.2
) and where we have chosen
\varepsilon>0
small enough depending on
\alpha
.

For the contributions
\text{X}_{2},\text{X}_{3}
, we use that
\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)
is real (by radial symmetry). We find

\begin{split}\text{X}_{2}&=\frac{1}{4}\left\|\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}[\varphi_{0},-\Delta](-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{HS}}^{2},\\
\text{X}_{3}&=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left\|\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}\nabla\varphi_{0}\right\|_{\text{HS}}^{2}.\end{split}

Proceeding as above, it is then straightforward to see that
|\text{X}_{2}|,|\text{X}_{3}|\leq C
and, using the bounds
\|\nabla\varphi_{0}\|_{op}\leq C
,
\|[\varphi_{0},-\Delta](-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1/2}\|_{op}\leq C
, moreover, that for any
\alpha<1/2
,

\begin{split}&\Big|\text{X}_{2}-\frac{1}{4}(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0})^{2}\left\|\varphi_{0}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}[\varphi_{0},-\Delta](-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1/2}\right\|_{\text{HS}}^{2}\Big|\leq CN^{-\alpha},\\
&\Big|\text{X}_{3}-\frac{1}{4}(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0})^{2}\left\|\varphi_{0}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}\nabla\varphi_{0}\right\|_{\text{HS}}^{2}\Big|\leq CN^{-\alpha}.\end{split}

To bound
\text{X}_{4}
, we write

\begin{split}\text{X}_{4}&=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left[\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}\big(V_{\text{ext}}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\varphi_{0}^{2}-\varepsilon_{\text{GP}}\big)\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2}}\varphi_{0}\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)\varphi_{0}\right].\\
\end{split}

Thus

|\text{X}_{4}|\leq C\big\|\varphi_{0}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}(V_{\text{ext}}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}\varphi_{0}^{2}-\varepsilon_{\text{GP}})\big\|_{\text{HS}}\big\|(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{-1}\varphi_{0}\big\|_{\text{HS}}.

Since
V_{\text{ext}}
grows at most exponentially by (
1.7
) and, by (
A.1
),
\varphi_{0}
decays faster than any exponential, choosing
\kappa>0
large enough we obtain that

\begin{split}\big\|\varphi_{0}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}&(V_{\text{ext}}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}\varphi_{0}^{2}-\varepsilon_{\text{GP}})\big\|_{\text{HS}}^{2}\\
&\leq C\int\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\frac{e^{-2\kappa|x-y|}}{|x-y|^{2}}\big[V_{\text{ext}}(y)+8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)-\varepsilon_{\text{GP}}\big]^{2}dxdy\leq C<\infty.\end{split}

From (
1.7
), we also have
\|(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{-1}\varphi_{0}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C
and thus
|\text{X}_{4}|\leq C
. Proceeding similarly as in (
3.40
) (and writing
\varphi_{0}(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}=(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}\varphi_{0}+(-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}[-\Delta,\varphi_{0}](-\Delta+\kappa^{2})^{-1}
), we conclude that

\Big|\text{X}_{4}-\frac{(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0})^{2}}{4}\operatorname{tr}\left[\varphi_{0}^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{-\Delta+\kappa^{2}}-\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2}}\Big)\varphi_{0}^{2}\right]\Big|\leq CN^{-\alpha}

for any
\alpha<1
. With (
1.7
), we can also show that
\text{X}_{5},\text{X}_{6}
are bounded and that

\begin{split}\Big|\text{X}_{5}-\frac{(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0})^{2}}{4\kappa^{2}}\|\varphi_{0}^{3}\|-\frac{(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0})^{2}}{4}\|(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{-1/2}Q\varphi_{0}^{3}\|^{2}\Big|&\leq CN^{-1}.\\
\Big|\text{X}_{6}+\frac{\kappa^{2}(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0})^{2}}{4}\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}\big[\varphi_{0}^{2}Q\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})}Q\varphi_{0}^{2}\Big]\Big|&\leq CN^{-\alpha}.\end{split}

for every
0<\alpha<1
. In fact, proceeding similarly as in (
3.40
) and using that
\|\widehat{Vf_{\ell}}(p/N)-\widehat{Vf_{\ell}}(0)|\leq C(|p|/N)^{\alpha}
and that
\||\nabla|^{\alpha}H^{-\alpha/2}_{\text{GP}}\|_{\text{op}}\leq C
, we can estimate

\begin{split}\Big|\text{X}_{6}+&\frac{\kappa^{2}(8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0})^{2}}{4}\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}\big[\varphi_{0}^{2}Q\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})}Q\varphi_{0}^{2}\Big]\Big|\\
&\leq\frac{C}{N^{\alpha}}\big\|H_{\text{GP}}^{\alpha/2}\varphi_{0}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{-3/4+\varepsilon}\big\|_{\text{HS}}\big\|H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{-1/4-\varepsilon}K_{N}\big\|_{\text{HS}}.\end{split}

The second norm on the r.h.s. is bounded, uniformly in
N
, by (
1.7
) (and by the exponential decay of
\varphi_{0}
, shown in (
A.1
)).To bound the first Hilbert-Schmidt norm, we write

\begin{split}H_{\text{GP}}^{\alpha/2}\varphi_{0}&\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{3/4-\varepsilon}}\\
&=\varphi_{0}\frac{H_{\text{GP}}^{\alpha/2}}{H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{3/4-\varepsilon}}\\
&+C\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\ s^{\alpha/2}\frac{1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}}[H_{\text{GP}},\varphi_{0}]\frac{1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}}\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{3/4-\varepsilon}}\end{split}

for an appropriate constant
C>0
and with
[H_{\text{GP}},\varphi_{0}]=-2\nabla\cdot\nabla\varphi_{0}-\Delta\varphi_{0}
.
Estimating
\|(s+H_{\text{GP}})^{-1}\nabla\|_{\text{op}}\leq C(1+s)^{1/2}
,
\|(s+H_{\text{GP}})^{-1}\|_{\text{op}}\leq C(1+s)^{-1}
and again
\|e^{-\alpha|x|}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+\kappa^{2})^{-1/4-\varepsilon}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C
, we obtain the desired bound.

Let us now consider the trace on the last line of (
3.37
). From the proof of Lemma
3.2
, recall that
\widetilde{D}^{1/2}=U_{0}^{*}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}e^{-\eta}
for some unitary
U_{0}:L^{2}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\rightarrow L^{2}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Thus (recall that by the proof of Lemma
3.1

a)
we have
H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\geq 0
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\right)-H_{\text{GP}}-K_{N}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\left(e^{-\eta}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}e^{\eta}+\text{h.c.}\right)-H_{\text{GP}}-K_{N}\Big].

Next we want to remove the factors of
e^{\pm\eta}
. If all operators were trace-class, we could use cyclicity of the trace. To overcome this issue, we rewrite it as

\begin{split}&\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\left[e^{-\eta}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}e^{\eta}-H_{\text{GP}}-K_{N}\right]+\text{h.c.}\right)\\
&=\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\Big(\Big[H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left(\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}-K_{N}\\
&\hskip 48.36958pt+\left(e^{-\eta}-1\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left(\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}\left(e^{\eta}-1\right)\\
&\hskip 48.36958pt+\left(e^{-\eta}-1\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}\\
&\hskip 48.36958pt+H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}\left(e^{-\eta}-1\right)\\
&\hskip 48.36958pt+\left(e^{-\eta}-1+\eta\right)H_{\text{GP}}\left(e^{\eta}-1-\eta\right)+\left(1-\eta\right)H_{\text{GP}}\left(e^{-\eta}-1-\eta\right)\\
&\hskip 48.36958pt+\left(e^{-\eta}-1+\eta\right)H_{\text{GP}}(1+\eta)-\eta H_{\text{GP}}\eta\Big]+\text{h.c.}\Big).\end{split}

(3.41)

Now we want to move all factors involving
\eta
to the right. For this we will use that for all
\varepsilon>0
we have

\begin{split}&\|QH_{\text{GP}}^{1/2-\varepsilon}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}Q\|_{\text{HS}}<\infty,\\
&\|QH_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2-\varepsilon}Q\|_{\text{HS}}<\infty.\end{split}

(3.42)

Before proving (
3.42
), we show how we apply it to (
3.41
). For the term on the second line of the r.h.s of (
3.41
) we can use (
3.42
), the boundedness of
e^{-\eta}-1
and
\|QH_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon}(e^{\eta}-1)\|_{\text{HS}}\leq e^{\|\eta\|_{\text{op}}}\|H_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon-1}Q\|_{\text{op}}\|H_{\text{GP}}\eta\|_{\text{HS}}<\infty
for
0\leq\varepsilon\leq 1
to get

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\left(e^{-\eta}-1\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left(\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2-\varepsilon}H_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon}\left(e^{\eta}-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left(\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}\left(e^{\eta}-1\right)\left(e^{-\eta}-1\right)\right).

Using additionally
\|H_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon}(e^{-\eta}-1)H_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq\|H_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon}\eta H_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{\text{HS}}+e^{\|\eta\|_{\text{op}}}\|H_{\text{GP}}\eta\|_{\text{HS}}^{2}<\infty
for
0\leq\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\left(e^{-\eta}-1\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(H_{\text{GP}}^{-\varepsilon}\left(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2-\varepsilon}\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon}(e^{-\eta}-1)H_{\text{GP}}^{\varepsilon}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}\left(e^{-\eta}-1\right)\right).

Now we write
e^{-\eta}-1+\eta=g_{\eta}\eta^{2}
, where
g_{\eta}
is a bounded operator that commutes with
\eta
. Using that
\eta
and
\eta H_{\text{GP}}
are both Hilbert-Schmidt, we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\left(e^{-\eta}-1+\eta\right)H_{\text{GP}}(1+\eta)\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\eta H_{\text{GP}}\eta g_{\eta}(1+\eta)\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(H_{\text{GP}}(1+\eta)\left(e^{-\eta}-1+\eta\right)\right).

The remaining terms can be dealt with in a similar fashion. Thus, once we have shown (
3.42
) we obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}

\displaystyle\left(\left[e^{-\eta}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left(\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}e^{\eta}-K_{N}\right]+\text{h.c.}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\left[H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left(\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right)H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}-K_{N}\right]+\text{h.c.}\right).

Let us now prove (
3.42
).
With the integral representation

x^{1/2}=x\cdot x^{-1/2}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{ds}{\sqrt{s}}\frac{x}{s+x}=\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\Lambda}\frac{ds}{\sqrt{s}}-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\Lambda}ds\,\sqrt{s}\frac{1}{s+x}

we can write

\begin{split}\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}&+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\\
&=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\left[\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}+2H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}K_{N}H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}}-\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}\right]\\
&=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}+2H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}K_{N}H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}}.\end{split}

(3.43)

Using the resolvent identiy, we obtain

\displaystyle QH_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2-\varepsilon}Q

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,Q\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}K_{N}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{-\varepsilon}Q

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{4}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,Q\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\frac{1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}+2H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}\frac{H_{\text{GP}}^{-\varepsilon}}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}Q.

Using that
\|Q[K_{N},H_{N}]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}Q\|_{\text{HS}}<\infty
we obtain the second inequality in (
3.42
). Similarly one obtains the other inequality in (
3.42
).
Therefore, we can use cyclicity and get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}+\widetilde{D}^{-1/2}\widetilde{E}\widetilde{D}^{1/2}\right)-H_{\text{GP}}-K_{N}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\left(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}+\text{h.c.}\right)-H_{\text{GP}}-K_{N}\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}\Big[\left(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}-K_{N}\right)+\text{h.c.}\Big].

Applying the resolvent identity twice to (
3.42
), we obtain

\begin{split}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}&[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}-K_{N}\\
=\;&\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,H_{\text{GP}}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}K_{N}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}-K_{N}\\
&-\frac{4}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}\\
&+\frac{8}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\Big[\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\Big]^{3}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}+2H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}K_{N}H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}.\end{split}

(3.44)

Since

\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\frac{H_{\text{GP}}}{(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{2}}=1

the trace of the first line on the r.h.s. of (
3.44
) vanishes, by cyclicity. To apply cyclicity of the trace, we need here to be a bit careful, since
K_{N}
is only guaranteed to be trace class, if
\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)
is integrable, a property which does not follow from our assumptions (
1.7
) on the interaction
V
.
In general, we can justify cyclicity remarking that, for self-adjoint operators
A,B
, we have the identity

2ABA-A^{2}B-BA^{2}=-\big[A,\big[A,B\big]\big].

Thus

\begin{split}&\left(\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,H_{\text{GP}}\frac{1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}K_{N}\frac{1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}-K_{N}\right)+\text{h.c.}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt=\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(H_{\text{GP}}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1}-K_{N}\right)-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,H_{\text{GP}}\Big[\frac{1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}},\Big[\frac{1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}},K_{N}\Big]\Big]\right)+\text{h.c.}\end{split}

A tedious but straightforward analysis shows that the r.h.s. of the last equation is trace class and that its trace norm depends continuously on
Vf_{\ell}
in the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
topology. For this argument we use that
(\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\varphi_{0})
has exponential decay which can be proved similarly like (
A.1
) and (
A.2
). With a simple approximation argument, and cyclicity of the trace for the second and third term in (
3.44
), we can therefore conclude that

\begin{split}\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}&\Big[\left(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\left[\big(H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(H_{\text{GP}}+2K_{N})H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\big)^{1/2}-H_{\text{GP}}\right]H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}-K_{N}\right)+\text{h.c.}\Big]\\
=&-\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}\\
&+\frac{4}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}\Big[\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\Big]^{3}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}+2H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}K_{N}H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}}.\end{split}

(3.45)

To handle the first term on the r.h.s. we use cyclicity of the trace (from (
1.7
), it follows that
\|K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{-3/4-\varepsilon}Q\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C
for any
\varepsilon>0
, and therefore the operator under consideration is of trace class, justifying cyclicity) and the fact that

\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\sqrt{s}\,\frac{1}{(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{3}}=\frac{1}{4H_{\text{GP}}^{3}}

to write

\begin{split}-\frac{2}{\pi}&\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\sqrt{s}\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}\\
=\;&-\frac{1}{4}\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}K_{N}\frac{1}{H_{\text{GP}}}K_{N}-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\sqrt{s}\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}\frac{H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}\Big[\Big[K_{N},\frac{H_{\text{GP}}}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}\Big],K_{N}\Big]\frac{H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}.\end{split}

(3.46)

The first term on the r.h.s. cancels precisely with the main contribution on the r.h.s. of (
3.38
). Expanding the commutators in the second term, we generate several contributions. All these contributions are bounded, uniformly in
N
. As an example, we estimate the term

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}&ds\sqrt{s}\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}\frac{H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}\Big[K_{N},H_{\text{GP}}\Big]\frac{1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}\Big[K_{N},H_{\text{GP}}\Big]\frac{H_{\text{GP}}^{3/2}}{(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{2}}\Big|\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\Big\|\frac{H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}e^{-\alpha|x|}\Big\|_{\text{HS}}\Big\|e^{\alpha|x|}[K_{N},H_{\text{GP}}]\frac{1}{(H_{\text{GP}}+1)^{1/2}}\Big\|_{\text{op}}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\Big\|\frac{H_{\text{GP}}+1}{s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2}}\Big\|_{\text{op}}\Big\|\frac{1}{(H_{\text{GP}}+1)^{1/2}}[K_{N},H_{\text{GP}}]e^{\alpha|x|}\Big\|_{\text{op}}\Big\|e^{-\alpha|x|}\frac{H^{3/2}_{\text{GP}}}{(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{2}}\Big\|_{\text{HS}}\\
&\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\frac{1}{(1+s)^{7/4-2\varepsilon}}\leq C\end{split}

(3.47)

choosing
\varepsilon>0
small enough. Here we used that, for any
\alpha>0
,
\|e^{\alpha|x|}[K_{N},H_{\text{GP}}](H_{\text{GP}}+1)^{-1/2}\|_{\text{op}}\leq C
(notice that the Laplacian commutes with the multiplier
\widehat{Vf_{\ell}}(./N)
in
K_{N}
; when commuted through the multiplication operator
\varphi_{0}
, it produces terms that can be controlled by
(H_{\text{GP}}+1)^{-1/2}
and decay faster than
e^{\alpha|x|}
in space, by Lemma
A.2
) and that, from (
1.7
),
\|H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{-1}e^{-\alpha|x|}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C(1+s)^{-3/8+\varepsilon}
,
\|(1+H_{\text{GP}})/(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})\|_{\text{op}}\leq C(1+s)^{-1/2}
,
\|e^{-\alpha|x|}H_{\text{GP}}^{3/2}/(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{2}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C(1+s)^{-7/8+\varepsilon}
. Proceeding similarly, we can control all other contributions arising from the second term on the r.h.s. of (
3.46
). Arguing analogously to (
3.40
), we can also show that, as
N\to\infty
, these contributions approach the expressions obtained replacing
\widehat{Vf_{\ell}}(./N)
by
8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}
. Here the convergence has a rate proportional to
N^{-\alpha}
, for any
\alpha<1
. This is related to the fact that the integral on the last line of (
3.47
) remains finite if we insert a factor
s^{\alpha/4}
, for any
\alpha<1
, and on the observation that, since
|\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(p/N)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}|\leq C(|p|/N)^{\alpha}
, a rate
N^{-\alpha}
corresponds to
|p|^{\alpha}\simeq H_{\text{GP}}^{\alpha/2}
and thus exactly to a factor
s^{\alpha/4}
.

Finally, let us consider the second term on the r.h.s. of (
3.45
). We can bound it by

\begin{split}\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\operatorname{tr}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}&\Big[\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}\Big]^{3}\frac{1}{s+H^{2}_{\text{GP}}+2H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}K_{N}H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}}\Big|\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}ds\,\sqrt{s}\,\frac{1}{(1+s)^{7/4-2\varepsilon}}\leq C\end{split}

because
\|e^{\alpha|x|}K_{N}\|_{\text{op}}\leq C
,
\|H_{\text{GP}}^{1/2}K_{N}H_{\text{GP}}^{-1/2}\|_{\text{op}}\leq C
(controlling the commutator between
H_{\text{GP}}
and
K_{N}
as explained in the previous paragraph) and
\|H^{1/2}_{\text{GP}}(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{-1}e^{-\alpha|x|}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C(1+s)^{-3/8+\varepsilon}
,
\|H_{\text{GP}}(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{-1}e^{-\alpha|x|}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C(1+s)^{-1/8+\varepsilon}
,
\|H_{\text{GP}}^{3/2}(s+H_{\text{GP}}^{2})^{-1}\|_{\text{op}}\leq C(1+s)^{-1/4}
. Also in this case, we can show that as
N\to\infty
, this term approaches
the expression obtained replacing
\widehat{(Vf_{\ell})}(./N)
by
8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}
, with a rate of order
N^{-\alpha}
, for
\alpha<1
.

Collecting all bounds for the terms
\text{X}_{1},\dots,\text{X}_{6}
and for the contributions on the r.h.s. of (
3.45
) (after removing the first term on the r.h.s. of (
3.46
)), we obtain the formula (
1.8
) for the ground state energy, with
E_{\text{Bog}}
as on the r.h.s. of (
1.11
). It is easy to check that the arguments of this section remain true if we replace the potential
\widehat{Vf_{\ell}}(./N)
by the approximate identity
1\!\!1_{\delta}
(corresponding, in Fourier space, to the multiplier with the Gaussian
e^{-\delta p^{2}/2}
) and we let
\delta\to 0
. This shows the validity of (
1.9
) and concludes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
∎

4
Analysis of
\mathcal{G}_{N}

In this section we analyze
\mathcal{G}_{N}
, defined in (
2.24
). Motivated by (
2.3
), we write

\mathcal{G}_{N}=\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(0)}+\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(1)}+\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(2)}+\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(3)}+\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(4)},

where, for
j\in\{0,1,2,3,4\}
, we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(j)}=e^{-B}\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(j)}e^{B}.

In the following, we will analyze the operators
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(j)}
separately; the results will be combined in Sec.
4.5
to conclude the proof of Proposition
2.5
. The strategy used to show Prop.
2.5
is very similar to the one used in
[
10
, Sec. 6]
,
[
6
, Sec. 7]
and
[
11
, Sec. 6]
, so we outline the key steps only. We focus in particular on the operator bounds that lead to (
2.26
) and (
2.34
). The commutator bounds (
2.27
) can be proved in the same way, following the remarks at the beginning of
[
6
, Sec. 7]
. We omit the details, because this would only lead to additional notation.

Before we start with the analysis of the operators
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(j)}
, let us record the following lemma that gives a rough bound on the conjugation of the kinetic and potential energies.

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume (
1.7
). For every
j\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

e^{-B}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}e^{B}\leq C\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}+CN(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j+1}

(4.1)

and

e^{-B}\mathcal{V}_{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}e^{B}\leq C\mathcal{V}_{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}+CN(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}.

(4.2)

Similarly, for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C>0
such that

e^{-B}\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}e^{B}\leq C(\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}.

(4.3)

Proof.

For simplicity, let us focus on the case
j=0
. The general case is then a straightforward generalization of the argument (see also
[
6
, Lemma 7.1]
for the analogous statements in the translation invariant setting).

Let
\xi\in\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
be normalized and define
s\mapsto\xi_{s}
by
\xi_{s}=\langle\xi,e^{-sB}\mathcal{K}e^{sB}\xi\rangle
. The key estimate to obtain the bound (
4.1
) is the operator bound

\begin{split}\pm[\mathcal{K},B]=\pm\int dx\,\big(\nabla_{x}b^{*}_{x}b^{*}(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x})+\text{h.c.}\big)\leq\mathcal{K}+CN(\mathcal{N}+1),\end{split}

where we used that
\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}\leq CN
, by (
2.13
). This implies with Lemma
2.3
that

|\partial_{s}\xi_{s}|\leq\xi_{s}+CN\langle\xi,(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle

and (
4.1
) follows from Gronwall’s lemma. Similarly, (
4.2
) is based on
\|\eta\|_{\infty}\leq CN
and

\begin{split}\pm[\mathcal{V}_{N},B]=&\,\pm\frac{1}{2}\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-y))\big(\eta(x;y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}+2b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}a^{*}(\eta_{x})a_{y}+\text{h.c.}\big)\\
\leq&\,C(\mathcal{V}_{N}+N)\end{split}

and the last bound (
4.3
) follows from

\pm[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},B]=\pm\bigg(\int dx\ V_{\text{ext}}(x)b^{*}_{x}b^{*}(\eta_{x})+\text{h.c.}\bigg)\leq C(\mathcal{N}+1).

Here, we used that
\|V_{\text{ext}}\varphi_{0}\|\leq C
, which follows form the assumption in (
1.7
) that
V_{\text{ext}}
has at most exponential growth, the estimate
\|\eta_{x}\|\leq C\varphi_{0}(x)
and the fact that
\varphi_{0}
has exponential decay with arbitrary rate, by Eq. (
A.1
).
∎

Through the rest of this section, we assume that
V
and
V_{\text{ext}}
satisfy the assumptions (
1.7
).

4.1
Analysis of
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(0)}
and
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(1)}

Lemma 4.2
.

We have that

\begin{split}e^{-B}\mathcal{N}e^{B}&=\int dx\,\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+b(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})\big]+\|\sigma\|^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{N}},\end{split}

where the self-adjoint operator
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{N}}
satisfies
\pm\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{N}}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}
.

Proof.

Observing that

\mathcal{N}=\int dx\,a^{*}_{x}a_{x}=\int dx\,b^{*}_{x}b_{x}+\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{N}-1)/N,

the claim follows from the decomposition (
2.19
), the bound (
2.21
) and Lemma
2.3
.
∎

Corollary 4.3
.

We have that

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(0)}&=N\big\langle\varphi_{0},\big(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}+(N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0})\varphi_{0}\big)\varphi_{0}\big\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{V}(0)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-\Big(\big\langle\varphi_{0},\big(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}\big)\varphi_{0}\rangle+\widehat{V}(0)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}\Big)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\bigg(\int dx\,\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+b(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})\big]+\|\sigma\|^{2}\bigg)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(0)},\end{split}

where the self-adjoint operator
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(0)}
satisfies
\pm\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(0)}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}
.

Proof.

We notice that

\Big|\int dxdy\,N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)-\widehat{V}(0)\int dx\,\varphi_{0}^{4}(x)\Big|\leq CN^{-1}

for some
C=C(\|\varphi_{0}\|_{H^{1}},\||\cdot|V\|_{1})>0
and the rest follows from (
4.2
) and Lemma
2.3
.
∎

To analyze
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(1)}
, let us define
h_{N}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
by

h_{N}=\big(N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N.)\ast|\varphi_{0}|^{2}\big)\varphi_{0}.

(4.4)

The proof of the following proposition is a straightforward adaption of
[
11
, Prop. 6.2]
and will therefore be omitted.

Lemma 4.4
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(1)}=&\,\big[\sqrt{N}b(\gamma(h_{N}))+\sqrt{N}b^{*}(\sigma(h_{N}))+\emph{h.c.}\big]+\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(1)},\end{split}

where the self-adjoint operator
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(1)}
satisfies
\pm\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(1)}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}
.

4.2
Analysis of
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(2)}

In this section, we study
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(2)}=e^{-B}\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(2)}e^{B}
. We split the operator
\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(2)}
introduced in (
2.3
) acoording to

\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(2)}=:\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(2,V)}.

We start with the conjugation of
\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}
.

Lemma 4.5
.

We have that

\begin{split}e^{-B}\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}e^{B}&=\int dx\,V_{\text{ext}}(x)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+b(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\operatorname{tr}(\sigma V_{\text{ext}}\sigma)+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}}\end{split}

for an error
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}}
that satisfies
\pm\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

Proof.

The proof is a simple consequence of Cauchy-Schwarz, Lemma
2.4
, the bounds
\|\eta^{(n)}_{x}\|\leq C\varphi_{0}(x)
for
n\geq 1
, the fact that
\varphi_{0}
has exponential decay with arbitrary rate, by Eq. (
A.1
), and that
V_{\text{ext}}
grows at most exponentially, by Eq. (
1.7
).
∎

We continue with the conjugation of
\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(2,V)}
which reads by definition

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(2,V)}&=\int dxdy\;N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\Big(b_{x}^{*}b_{x}-\frac{1}{N}a_{x}^{*}a_{x}\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int dxdy\;N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\Big(b_{x}^{*}b_{y}-\frac{1}{N}a_{x}^{*}a_{y}\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\;N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\varphi_{0}(x)\Big(b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}+b_{x}b_{y}\Big).\end{split}

Lemma 4.6
.

Set
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(2,V)}=e^{-B}\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(2,V)}e^{B}
. Then we have that

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(2,V)}=&\,\int dx\,\big(N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}\big)(x)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\\
&\qquad+b(\sigma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&+\int dxdy\,N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b(\gamma_{x})\\
&\qquad+b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+b(\sigma_{y})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b^{*}(\gamma_{x})\\
&\qquad+b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+b(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+\emph{h.c.}\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\big[\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big)d_{\eta,y}^{*}+d_{\eta,x}^{*}\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big)+\emph{h.c.}]\\
&+\operatorname{tr}\big(\sigma\big[N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}+N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big]\sigma\big)\\
&+\operatorname{tr}\big(\gamma\big[N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big]\sigma)+\big(\widehat{V}(0)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\big)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}\,\mathcal{N}+\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(2,V)}\end{split}

for an error
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(2,V)}
that satisfies

\pm\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(2,V)}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

Here, we view
N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}
as multiplication operator in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and we identify, by slight abuse of notation,
N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)
with its associated Hilbert-Schmidt operator in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
.

Proof.

Let us outline the main steps. First of all, we have

\begin{split}&\pm\bigg(\int dxdy\;N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)a_{x}^{*}a_{x}+\int dxdy\;N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)a_{x}^{*}a_{y}\bigg)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1),\\
\end{split}

so that these contributions can be neglected, by Lemma
2.3
. Similarly, the remaining diagonal terms are easily seen to be equal to

\begin{split}&\int dxdy\;N^{3}V(N(x-y))\big[\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)e^{-B}b_{x}^{*}b_{x}e^{B}+\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)e^{-B}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}e^{B}\big]\\
&=\int dx\,\big(N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}\big)(x)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\\
&\qquad+b(\sigma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int dxdy\,N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b(\gamma_{x})\\
&\hskip 34.14322pt+b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+b(\sigma_{y})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\operatorname{tr}\big(\sigma\big[N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}+N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big]\sigma\big)+\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(21,V)}\end{split}

for an error
\pm\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(21,V)}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}
. This follows from the decomposition (
2.19
) and the bounds from Lemma
2.4
. The pairing term can be treated similarly. We compute

\begin{split}e^{-B}b^{*}_{x}b^{*}_{y}e^{B}&=b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+b(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle-N^{-1}\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle\,\mathcal{N}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big)d_{\eta,y}^{*}+d_{\eta,x}^{*}\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-N^{-1}a^{*}(\gamma_{y})a(\sigma_{x})+d^{*}_{\eta,x}d^{*}_{\eta,y}.\end{split}

Now, we observe that

\pm\bigg(\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big(\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle-k(x;y)\big)\mathcal{N}\bigg)\leq CN^{-1}\mathcal{N},

because
|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle-k(x;y)|\leq C\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)
(recall the definition (
2.20
) and the bounds (
2.14
), (
2.15
)). Since
N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N.)
is an approximation of Dirac’s measure of mass
\big(\widehat{V}(0)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\big)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1})
, by Eq. (
2.8
), we then find

\begin{split}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)(-k(x;y))&=\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\\
&=\big(\widehat{V}(0)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\big)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}),\end{split}

where we used that
\varphi_{0}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Similarly, we bound with Cauchy-Schwarz that

\pm\bigg(\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)a^{*}(\gamma_{y})a(\sigma_{x})+\text{h.c.}\bigg)\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)

and from the bound (
2.23
), it is simple to see that for all
\xi\in\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
we have

\begin{split}&\int dxdy\,N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)|\langle\xi,d^{*}_{\eta,x}d^{*}_{\eta,y}\xi\rangle|\\
&\leq C\int dxdy\,NV(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|\Big[\|\eta_{x}\|\|\eta_{y}\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\xi\|\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\|\eta\||\eta(x;y)|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|+\|\eta\|\|\eta_{y}\||\|a_{x}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2)}\xi\|\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\|\eta\|\|\eta_{x}\|\|a_{y}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\xi\|+\|\eta\|^{2}\|a_{x}a_{y}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|\Big]\\
&\leq CN^{-1}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle.\end{split}

Collecting all terms and using the previous bounds, we conclude the proof of the lemma.
∎

Finally, let us analyze the conjugation of the kinetic energy
\mathcal{K}
under
e^{B}
. We start with the following preparation.

Lemma 4.7
.

Assume (
1.7
). Then, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
n,m\geq 1
, we have that

\displaystyle\int dx\ a^{*}(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x}^{(n)})a(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x}^{(m)})\leq C^{n+m}(\mathcal{N}+1).

Moreover, recalling (
2.17
), (
2.19
) and setting
\overline{d}_{\eta,x}=d_{\eta,x}+(\mathcal{N}/N)b^{*}(\eta_{x})
, there exists for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
a constant
C>0
such that

\begin{split}&\int dx\ \|\sqrt{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{(n-1)/2}\nabla_{x}d_{s\eta,x}\xi\|^{2}\leq C\|(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{n/2}\xi\|^{2},\\
&\int dx\ \|\sqrt{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{(n-1)/2}\nabla_{x}\overline{d}_{s\eta,x}\xi\|^{2}\leq C\|(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{n/2}\xi\|^{2}\end{split}

(4.5)

and that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int dx\ \|\sqrt{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{(n-1)/2}d_{s\eta}(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x})\xi\|^{2}\leq C\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{(n+1)/2}\xi\|^{2},\\
&\int dx\ \|\sqrt{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{(n-1)/2}d_{s\eta}^{*}(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x})\xi\|^{2}\leq C\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{(n+1)/2}\xi\|^{2}\end{split}

for all
\xi\in\mathcal{F}^{\leq N}
and all
s\in[0;1]
.

Lemma
4.7
is a slight generalization of Lemma
[
11
, Lemma 4.7]
and can be proved in the same way, based on the kernel bounds from Lemma
2.2
; we omit the details.

Lemma 4.8
.

We have that

\begin{split}&e^{-B}\mathcal{K}e^{B}\\
&=\int dx\,\big[\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\gamma_{x})\nabla_{x}b(\gamma_{x})+\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\gamma_{x})\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+\nabla_{x}b(\sigma_{x})\nabla_{x}b(\gamma_{x})+\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\nabla_{x}b(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int dx\,\big[b(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x})\nabla_{x}d_{\eta,x}+\emph{h.c.}\big]+\|\nabla_{1}\sigma\|^{2}(1-\mathcal{N}/N)+N^{-1}\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}}{N}\Big(\int dx\,\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+b(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})\big]+\|\sigma\|^{2}\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\end{split}

where the error
\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}
satisfies

\pm\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{K}}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}^{2}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

Proof.

The proof is a straightforward adapation of
[
6
, Lemma 7.2]
to the setting of trapped particles, so let us focus on the main steps. We start with the identity

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}&=\int dx\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}\nabla_{x}b_{x}+N^{-1}\int dxdy\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{y}\nabla_{x}b_{x}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+N^{-2}(\mathcal{N}-1)\int dx\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}\nabla_{x}b_{x}+N^{-2}\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\end{split}

(4.6)

and notice that, by the rough bound (
4.1
), the last two terms are errors bounded by

\pm N^{-2}e^{-B}\bigg((\mathcal{N}-1)\int dx\,\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}\nabla_{x}b_{x}+\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\bigg)e^{B}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}^{2}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

Hence, let us consider the remaining two terms on the right hand side in (
4.6
), starting with the first term. We apply the decomposition (
2.19
) and find that

\displaystyle\int dx\,e^{-B}\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}\nabla_{x}b_{x}e^{B}=E_{1}+E_{2}+E_{3}+\int dx\big[b(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x})\nabla_{x}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\big],

where

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle=\int dx\ (\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x}))(\nabla_{x}b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})),

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle=\int dx\ (\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\nabla_{x}(\sigma_{x}-\eta_{x})))\nabla_{x}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.},

\displaystyle E_{3}

\displaystyle=\int dx\ \nabla_{x}d_{\eta,x}^{*}\nabla_{x}d_{\eta,x}.

The contributions
E_{2}
and
E_{3}
are error terms. This follows from (
4.5
), which implies

\displaystyle 0\leq E_{2}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+\mathcal{K}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)

and, using in addition the bounds from Lemma
2.2
, that

\displaystyle|\langle\xi,E_{3}\xi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1/2}\Big[\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\xi\|+\sum_{m\geq 2}\frac{1}{m!}\|\nabla_{1}\eta^{(m)}\|\cdot\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\|(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-1/2}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{N}+\mathcal{K}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle.

We are left with
E_{1}
. Using the standard commutation relations,

\displaystyle E_{1}

\displaystyle=\int dx\big[\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\gamma_{x})\nabla_{x}b(\gamma_{x})+\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+b(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})\nabla_{x}b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})b(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\|\nabla_{1}\sigma\|^{2}(1-\mathcal{N}/N)-\frac{1}{N}\int dx\,a^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})a(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x}).

By Lemma
4.7
, the last term on the right hand side is an error term bounded by

\displaystyle 0\leq N^{-1}\int dx\,a^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})a(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)

and therefore we conclude that

\begin{split}&\int dx\,e^{-B}\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}\nabla_{x}b_{x}e^{B}\\
&=\int dx\,\big[\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\gamma_{x})\nabla_{x}b(\gamma_{x})+\nabla_{x}b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})+b(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})\nabla_{x}b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})b(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int dx\,\big[b(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x})\nabla_{x}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\big]+\|\nabla_{1}\sigma\|^{2}(1-\mathcal{N}/N)+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{K}},\end{split}

for an error
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{K}}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{N}+\mathcal{K}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
. It remains to analyze the second term on the right hand side of (
4.6
). A straightforward adaption of the analysis in
[
6
, Eq. (7.18) to (7.20)]
shows that this term is given by

\begin{split}&N^{-1}\int dxdy\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{y}\nabla_{x}b_{x}\\
&=N^{-1}\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}\Big(\int dx\,\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+b(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})\big]+\|\sigma\|^{2}\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+N^{-1}\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{K}}\end{split}

for an error
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}^{2}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
. Combining the two last identities and collecting the error terms concludes the claim.
∎

4.3
Analysis of
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(3)}

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
h_{N}=\big(N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N.)\ast|\varphi_{0}|^{2}\big)\varphi_{0}
, as in Eq. (
4.4
). Then, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(3)}&=\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\Big(b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]+\emph{h.c.}\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-\big[\sqrt{N}b(\gamma(h_{N}))+\sqrt{N}b^{*}(\sigma(h_{N}))+\emph{h.c.}\big]+\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(3)}\end{split}

for an error
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(3)}
that satisfies

\displaystyle\pm\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(3)}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

Proof.

We recall (
2.3
) and use
a_{y}^{*}a_{x}=b_{y}^{*}b_{x}+N^{-1}a_{y}^{*}\mathcal{N}a_{x}
to write
\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(3)}
as

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{N}^{(3)}&=\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{x}+\text{h.c.}\big]\\
&\quad+\int dxdy\,N^{3/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}\,\mathcal{N}a_{x}+\text{h.c.}\big].\end{split}

(4.7)

The contribution arising from the second term on the right hand side of the last equation is an error. Indeed, we infer from Cauchy-Schwarz and Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle\int dxdy\,N^{3/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\big|\langle e^{B}\xi,b_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}\mathcal{N}a_{x}e^{B}\xi\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq CN^{-1}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{V}_{N}+N)(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle^{1/2}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\xi\rangle^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq CN^{-1/2}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle.

As for the first contribution from the r.h.s. of (
4.7
), we decompose it with (
2.19
) into
the sum of the terms

\begin{split}M_{0}&=\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 19.91684pt\times\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big]\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]+\text{h.c.},\\
M_{1}&=\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\\
&\hskip 19.91684pt\times\Big(d_{\eta,x}^{*}\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big]\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt+\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big]d_{\eta,y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt+\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big]\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big]d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\Big),\\
M_{2}&=\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\Big(d_{\eta,x}^{*}d_{\eta,y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 19.91684pt+d_{\eta,x}^{*}\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big]d_{\eta,x}+\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big]d_{\eta,y}^{*}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\Big),\\
M_{3}&=\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)d_{\eta,x}^{*}d_{\eta,y}^{*}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\end{split}

(4.8)

Notice that the index
i
in
M_{i}
counts the number of
d_{\eta}
-operators it contains.

The operators
M_{1},M_{2}
and
M_{3}
are error terms. Let us illustrate this for
M_{1}
, the contributions
M_{2}
and
M_{3}
can be handled analogously. If we apply Cauchy-Schwarz together with the bounds (
2.21
), (
2.22
) and Lemma
2.2
, we find that

\begin{split}&\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)|\langle\xi,\!d_{\eta,x}^{*}(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})\!+\!b(\sigma_{y}))(b(\gamma_{x})\!+\!b^{*}\!(\sigma_{x}))\xi\rangle|\\
&\,\leq C\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\big[\|\eta_{y}\|\|d_{\eta,x}\xi\|+\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{-1/2}a_{y}d_{\eta,x}\xi\|\big]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\big[\|\eta_{x}\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|+\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}a_{x}\xi\|\big]\\
&\,\leq C\int dxdy\,N^{3/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\big[\|\eta_{x}\|\|\eta_{y}\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|+CN\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt+\|a_{x}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|+\|\eta_{x}\|\|a_{y}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|+\|a_{x}a_{y}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\big]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\big[\|\eta_{x}\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|+\|a_{x}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\big]\\
&\leq CN^{-1/2}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle.\end{split}

Notice that we used that
\|\eta\|_{\infty}\leq CN
and that
\|\eta_{x}\|\leq C\varphi_{0}(x)
. Similarly, we find that

\begin{split}&\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{-1/2}d_{\eta,y}(b(\gamma_{x})\!+\!b^{*}(\sigma_{x}))\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}(b(\gamma_{x})\!+\!b^{*}(\sigma_{x}))\xi\|\\
&+\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\|(b(\gamma_{y})\!+\!b^{*}(\sigma_{y}))(b(\gamma_{x})\!+\!b^{*}(\sigma_{x}))\xi\|\|d_{\eta,x}\xi\|\\
&\leq CN^{-1/2}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle,\end{split}

so that altogether
\pm(M_{1}+\text{h.c.})\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
. The contributions
M_{2}
and
M_{3}
can be controlled similarly, using additionally the bound (
2.23
). This shows

\pm(M_{2}+M_{3}+\text{h.c.})\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

Now, let us determine the main contributions to
M_{0}
, defined in (
4.8
). With

\begin{split}&\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big]\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big]\\
&=b^{*}_{x}b^{*}_{y}+\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle(1-\mathcal{N}/N)-N^{-1}a^{*}(\gamma_{y})a(\sigma_{x})\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+b^{*}_{x}\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{y})-b^{*}_{y}\big]+\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})-b^{*}_{x}\big]b^{*}(\gamma_{y})\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b(\sigma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})\\
\end{split}

and with the bounds (
2.14
), (
2.15
), it is simple to verify that

\begin{split}\pm&\bigg(M_{0}-\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt-\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]+\text{h.c.}\bigg)\\
&\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}.\end{split}

Notice that Lemma
2.2
also implies
|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle-k(x;y)|\leq C\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)
, with
k
defined in (
2.11
), so that we can simplify this further to

\begin{split}\pm&\bigg(M_{0}-\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 5.69046pt+\sqrt{N}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\varphi_{0}(x)\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]+\text{h.c.}\bigg)\\
&\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}.\end{split}

Finally, combining the estimates on
M_{0}
,
M_{1}
,
M_{2}
and
M_{3}
with the observation that

\begin{split}&\sqrt{N}\int dx\big(N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N.)\ast|\varphi_{0}|^{2}\big)\varphi_{0}(x)\big[b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&=\sqrt{N}b(\gamma(h_{N}))+\sqrt{N}b^{*}(\sigma(h_{N})),\end{split}

where
h_{N}=\big(N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N.)\ast|\varphi_{0}|^{2}\big)\varphi_{0}
, we conclude the proof of the lemma.
∎

4.4
Analysis of
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(4)}

Lemma 4.10
.

We have that

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(4)}&=\mathcal{V}_{N}+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle|^{2}\Big(1+\frac{1}{N}-\frac{2\mathcal{N}}{N}\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\gamma_{y})\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt+b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+b(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+\emph{h.c.}\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\varphi_{0}(x)\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\times\big[d_{\eta,x}^{*}(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y}))+(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x}))d_{\eta,y}^{*}+\emph{h.c.}\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell}^{2})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\bigg(\int du\,\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{u})b^{*}(\sigma_{u})+b^{*}(\gamma_{u})b(\gamma_{u})\\
&\hskip 62.59596pt+b^{*}(\sigma_{u})b(\sigma_{u})+b(\sigma_{u})b(\gamma_{u})\big]+\|\sigma_{u}\|^{2}\bigg)+\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(4)}\end{split}

for an error
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(4)}
that satisfies
\pm\mathcal{E}_{N}^{(4)}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
.

Proof.

We closely follow
[
6
, Lemma 7.4]
and outline the main steps. Using that

\displaystyle a_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}a_{y}a_{x}=b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{y}b_{x}\left(1-\frac{3}{N}+\frac{2\mathcal{N}}{N}\right)+a_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}a_{y}a_{x}\Theta(\mathcal{N})

for some function
\Theta:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{R}
that satisfies
\pm\Theta(\mathcal{N})\leq N^{-2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}
, we split

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(4)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\;N^{2}V(N(x-y))e^{-B}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{x}b_{y}\left(1-\frac{3}{N}+\frac{2\,\mathcal{N}}{N}\right)e^{B}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\;N^{2}V(N(x-y))e^{-B}a_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}a_{y}a_{x}\Theta(\mathcal{N})e^{B}.

Using the rough estimate (
4.2
) for the conjugation of
\mathcal{V}_{N}
, we immediately find that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\langle\xi,e^{-B}a_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}a_{y}a_{x}\Theta(\mathcal{N})e^{B}\xi\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\leq CN^{-1}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle.

We can therefore split
\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(4)}
into

\begin{split}\mathcal{G}_{N}^{(4)}&=\frac{N+1}{2N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))e^{-B}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{y}b_{x}e^{B}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{N}\int dudxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))e^{-B}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b^{*}_{u}b_{u}b_{y}b_{x}e^{B}+\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\end{split}

(4.9)

for some error
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
that satisfies
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
.

Now, we analyze the remaining two contributions on the right hand side in (
4.9
), starting with the one in the first line. Applying Eq. (
2.19
), we split this term into

\displaystyle\frac{(N+1)}{2N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))e^{-B}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{y}b_{x}e^{B}=V_{0}+V_{1}+V_{2}+V_{3}+V_{4},

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{0}&=\frac{(N+1)}{2N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b(\sigma_{y})+b(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt-N^{-1}\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle\,\mathcal{N}-N^{-1}a^{*}(\gamma_{y})a(\sigma_{x})\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\big[b(\gamma_{y})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt-N^{-1}\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle\,\mathcal{N}-N^{-1}a^{*}(\sigma_{x})a(\gamma_{y})\big],\\
\end{split}

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{1}&=\frac{(N+1)}{2N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\big[d_{\eta,x}^{*}(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y}))+(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x}))d_{\eta,y}^{*}]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\big[b(\gamma_{y})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y})b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})b(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y})b^{*}(\sigma_{x})+\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt-N^{-1}\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle\,\mathcal{N}-N^{-1}a^{*}(\sigma_{x})a(\gamma_{y})\big]+\text{h.c.},\end{split}

as well as

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{2}&=\frac{(N+1)}{2N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\big[(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x}))d_{\eta,y}^{*}(b(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y}))d_{\eta,x}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x}))d_{\eta,y}^{*}d_{\eta,y}(b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x}))\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+d_{\eta,x}^{*}(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y}))(b(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y}))d_{\eta,x}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+d_{\eta,x}^{*}(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y}))d_{\eta,y}(b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x}))\big]\\
&+\frac{(N+1)}{2N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\big[d_{\eta,x}^{*}d_{\eta,y}^{*}(b(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y}))(b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x}))+\text{ h.c.}\big],\\
V_{3}&=\frac{(N+1)}{2N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\big[(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x}))d_{\eta,y}^{*}+d_{\eta,x}^{*}(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big]d_{\eta,y}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.},\\
V_{4}&=\frac{(N+1)}{2N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))d_{\eta,x}^{*}d_{\eta,y}^{*}d_{\eta,y}d_{\eta,x}.\end{split}

The only relevant contributions to the energy are contained in
V_{0}
and
V_{1}
while
V_{2}
,
V_{3}
and
V_{4}
are negligible. To see this, let us start with
V_{4}
. Applying (
2.23
), we get

\displaystyle|\langle\xi,V_{4}\xi\rangle|
