\displaystyle\leq C\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\|d_{\eta,x}d_{\eta,y}\xi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))N^{-4}\big[\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\|a_{y}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{5/2}\xi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+N^{2}\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\xi\|^{2}+\|a_{x}a_{y}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\xi\|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq N^{-1}\langle\xi,(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle.

Similarly, we can use the bounds from Lemma
2.4
to show that

\pm V_{2},V_{3}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

It remains to extract the order one contributions to
V_{0}
and
V_{1}
. Recalling that, by Lemma
2.2
,
|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle-k(x;y)|\leq C\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)
we find that

\begin{split}V_{0}+V_{1}&=\mathcal{V}_{N}+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle|^{2}\Big(1+\frac{1}{N}-\frac{2\mathcal{N}}{N}\Big)\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt-\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b^{*}(\gamma_{y})\\
&\hskip 25.6073pt+b^{*}(\gamma_{y})b(\sigma_{x})+b^{*}(\gamma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+b(\sigma_{x})b(\sigma_{y})+\text{h.c.}\big]\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt-\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\varphi_{0}(x)\\
&\hskip 17.07182pt\times\big[d_{\eta,x}^{*}(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y}))+(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x}))d_{\eta,y}^{*}+\text{h.c.}\big]+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2},\end{split}

where the error
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
is such that
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
.

This concludes the analysis of the first term on the right hand side in (
4.9
). The second term is treated similarly and an adaption of the arguments from above yields

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{N}\int dudxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))e^{-B}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b^{*}_{u}b_{u}b_{y}b_{x}e^{B}\\
&=\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell}^{2})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\bigg(\int du\,\big[b^{*}(\gamma_{u})b^{*}(\sigma_{u})+b^{*}(\gamma_{u})b(\gamma_{u})\\
&\hskip 48.36958pt+b^{*}(\sigma_{u})b(\sigma_{u})+b(\sigma_{u})b(\gamma_{u})\big]+\|\sigma\|^{2}\bigg)+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{3}\end{split}

for some error
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{3}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
. The details are analogous to those in
[
6
, Lemma 7.4]
, taking into account the different setting; we omit the details.
∎

4.5
Proof of Proposition
2.5

In this section, we collect the results about
\mathcal{G}_{N}
and prove Proposition
2.5
. As mentioned at the beginning of this section, we will focus on the decomposition (
2.33
) and the bounds (
2.25
), (
2.34
); the commutator bounds (
2.27
) can be proved in the same way.

Combining the results of Lemmas
4.3
,
4.4
,
4.5
,
4.6
,
4.8
,
4.9
and
4.10
, we find that

\mathcal{G}_{N}=\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\int dxdy\,\Big[\widetilde{\Phi}(x;y)b^{*}_{x}b_{y}+\frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\Gamma}(x;y)\big(b^{*}_{x}b^{*}_{y}+b_{x}b_{y}\big)\Big]+\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\mathcal{V}_{N}+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}

(4.10)

where

\begin{split}\widetilde{\kappa}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}&=N\big\langle\varphi_{0},(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}+\widehat{V}(0)\varphi_{0}^{2}/2)\varphi_{0}\big\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\widehat{V}(0)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}+N^{-1}\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\operatorname{tr}\big(\sigma(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})\sigma\big)+\operatorname{tr}\big(\gamma\big[N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big]\sigma)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\operatorname{tr}\big(\sigma\big[N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}+N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big]\sigma\big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\big(1+N^{-1}\big)\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle|^{2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell}^{2})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\|\sigma\|^{2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+N^{-1}\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}\|\sigma\|^{2}-\big(\langle\varphi_{0},(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})\varphi_{0}\rangle+\widehat{V}(0)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}\big)\|\sigma\|^{2},\end{split}

where the operators
\widetilde{\Phi}
and
\widetilde{\Gamma}
are defined by

\begin{split}\widetilde{\Phi}&=\gamma\big(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}+N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}+N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big)\gamma\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+\sigma\big(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}+N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}+N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big)\sigma\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\gamma\big(N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big)\sigma+\text{h.c.}\Big)+\big(\widehat{V}(0)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\big)\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt-\big(\langle\varphi_{0},(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})\varphi_{0}\rangle+\widehat{V}(0)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}\big)(\gamma^{2}+\sigma^{2}),\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+2\Big(\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell}^{2})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)+N^{-1}\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}\Big)\sigma^{2},\\
\widetilde{\Gamma}&=\gamma\big(N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big)\gamma\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\sigma\!\big(N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big)\!\sigma\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\Big(\sigma\big(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}+N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0}^{2}+N^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big)\gamma+\text{h.c.}\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\Big(\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell}^{2})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\Big)\big(\gamma\sigma+\text{h.c.}\big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-\big(\langle\varphi_{0},(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})\varphi_{0}\rangle+\widehat{V}(0)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}-N^{-1}\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}\big)\big(\gamma\sigma+\text{h.c.}\big),\\
\end{split}

where
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}
is defined as in (
2.32
), where

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}&=\int dx\,\big[b(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x})\nabla_{x}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\big]\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\big[\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big)d_{\eta,y}^{*}+d_{\eta,x}^{*}\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{y})+b(\sigma_{y})\big)+\text{h.c.}\big]\\
\end{split}

and where the error
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}
satisfies the bound

\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{H}_{N}+\mathcal{N}^{2}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

Notice that here we used
\pm(\mathcal{N}-\int dx\,b_{x}^{*}b_{x})\leq N^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}
as well as

\begin{split}N^{-1}&\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))|\langle\sigma_{x},\gamma_{y}\rangle|^{2}\\
&=\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell}^{2})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}).\end{split}

Let us now focus on
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}
. We proceed here very similarly as in
[
6
, Section 7.5]
, so let us focus on the main steps. First of all, by the scattering equation (
2.7
), we see that

\begin{split}&\pm\Big(\int dx\,\big[b(\nabla_{x}\eta_{x})\nabla_{x}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\big]\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt-\int dxdy\,N^{3}(-\Delta f_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big[b_{y}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\big]\Big)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{H}_{N}+\mathcal{N}^{2}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).\end{split}

We can therefore write

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}

\displaystyle=\int dxdy\,N^{3}\big[-(\Delta f_{\ell})(N(x-y))+\frac{1}{2}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\big]\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)[b_{y}d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\big[b(\gamma_{y})d_{\eta,x}-b_{y}d_{\eta,x}+b^{*}(\sigma_{y})d_{\eta,x}+\text{h.c.}\big]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\big[d_{\eta,x}\big(b(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y})\big)+\text{h.c.}\big]+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}

\displaystyle=:\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{D}_{2}+\mathcal{D}_{3}+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}

for an error
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{H}_{N}+\mathcal{N}^{2}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
. The contributions
\mathcal{D}_{1}
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}
are easily seen to be small, using once more the scattering equation (
2.7
) together with the operator bounds from Lemma
2.4
. This yields that

\pm\mathcal{D}_{1},\pm\mathcal{D}_{2}\leq CN^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3},

Similarly, the term
\mathcal{D}_{3}
can be reduced to

\mathcal{D}_{3}=\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\big[d_{\eta,x}b^{*}(\eta_{y})+\text{h.c.}\big]+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2},

up to another error that satisfies
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{H}_{N}+\mathcal{N}^{2}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
. To extract the main contribution to this term, we adapt the arguments from
[
6
]
, using the identity

\begin{split}d_{\eta,x}&=\int_{0}^{1}ds\ d_{s\eta}^{*}(\eta_{x})-\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{1}ds\ e^{-sB}\mathcal{N}b^{*}(\eta_{x})e^{sB}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{1}ds\int dudy\ \eta(u;y)e^{-sB}b_{u}^{*}a_{y}^{*}a_{x}e^{sB}.\end{split}

Plugging this into
\mathcal{D}_{3}
, extracting the main contributions with the same tools as in the previous sections and collecting the previous bounds, one eventually finds that

\begin{split}\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}=&\,\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vf_{\ell}w_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\int dv\,\big[b^{*}(\sigma_{v})b^{*}(\gamma_{v})+b(\sigma_{v})b(\gamma_{v})+2b^{*}(\sigma_{v})b(\sigma_{v})\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell}f_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\|\sigma\|^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathcal{D}}\end{split}

for an error
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{D}}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{H}_{N}+\mathcal{N}^{2}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)
. Combining this term with the quadratic part in (
4.10
), we arrive at the identity (
2.33
) and the error bound (
2.34
). Notice that we use here Eq. (
2.7
) and the results from Lemma
2.1
which yields that

\begin{split}&\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vw_{\ell}^{2})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)+N^{-1}\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}-\langle\varphi_{0},(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})\varphi_{0}\rangle\\
&-\widehat{V}(0)\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}+\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(Vf_{\ell}w_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)\\
&=-\langle\varphi_{0},(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\varphi_{0}^{2})\varphi_{0}\rangle+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1})=-\varepsilon_{GP}+\mathcal{O}(N^{-1}).\end{split}

In particular, this proves part
b)
of Proposition
2.5
.

To prove the statements of part
a)
, we first note that, by
[
11
, Theorem 1.1]
, we have the lower bound
E_{N}\geq N\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\varphi_{0})-C
,
E_{N}
denoting the ground state energy of
H_{N}
. On the other hand, it is straightforward that the scattering equation (
2.7
) and Lemma
2.1
imply that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\kappa_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}=&\,N\langle\varphi_{0},(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}+(N^{3}V(N.)\ast\varphi_{0})\varphi_{0})\varphi_{0}\rangle+\|\nabla_{1}\eta\|^{2}\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,\big[2N^{3}(Vw_{\ell})(N(x-y))+N^{3}(Vw^{2}_{\ell})(N(x-y))\big]\varphi_{0}^{2}(x)\varphi_{0}^{2}(y)+\mathcal{O}(1)\\
=&\,N\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\varphi_{0})+\mathcal{O}(1).\end{split}

Hence, for
\Omega=(1,0,\dots,0)\in\mathcal{F}_{\bot\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
, we can use the trial state
U_{N}^{*}e^{B}\Omega\in L^{2}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3N})
to obtain the upper bound

E_{N}\leq\langle\Omega,e^{-B}U_{N}H_{N}U_{N}^{*}e^{B}\rangle=\langle\Omega,\mathcal{G}_{N}\Omega\rangle\leq N\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\varphi_{0})+C.

This proves that
|E_{N}-N\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\varphi_{0})|\leq C
for some constant
C>0
, independent of
N
.

Finally, it is simple to show that for any
\delta>0
, we have that

\pm(\mathcal{G}_{N}-\kappa_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}-\mathcal{H}_{N})\leq\delta\mathcal{H}_{N}+C\delta^{-1}(\mathcal{N}+1)

for some
C>0
. This follows directly from the decomposition (
2.33
), Cauchy-Schwarz and by observing that

\begin{split}\pm&\bigg(\int dxdy\,\Big[\Gamma(x;y)+\Delta\eta(x;y)-N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\Big]b_{x}b_{y}+\text{h.c.}\bigg)\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}+1)\end{split}

and that, by the scattering equation (
2.7
), we also have that

\pm\bigg(\int dxdy\,\Big[-\Delta\eta(x;y)+N^{3}(Vf_{\ell})(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)\Big]b_{x}b_{y}+\text{h.c.}\bigg)\leq C(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}+1).

The remaining contributions to
\mathcal{G}_{N}
are simple to control and we omit the details. Together with
\kappa_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}=E_{N}+\mathcal{O}(1)
, explained above, we thus conclude the proposition.

5
Analysis of
\mathcal{J}_{N}

The goal of this section is to prove Proposition
2.8
. To this end, we need to control the action of the operator
A
defined in (
2.45
) on the different parts of the Hamiltonian
\mathcal{G}_{N}
, which can be decomposed as in (
2.33
).

A first observation is that conjugation with
e^{A}
preserves, approximately, the number of excitations. The proof of the following Lemma is very similar to the proof of
[
11
, Lemma 2.6]
and will therefore be omitted.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
0<6\tau\leq\varepsilon
. Then, for every
j\in\mathbb{N}
, there is
C=C_{j,\varepsilon}>0
such that, for all
s\in[0;1]
, we have on
\mathcal{F}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
the estimate

\displaystyle e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}e^{sA}\leq C(\mathcal{N}+1)^{j}.

Another important point for our analysis, is that we can often replace
A
with the operator

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\left[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\right]-\text{h.c.}

where the
\widetilde{b}
,
\widetilde{b}^{*}
fields appearing in (
2.45
) have been replaced by
b,b^{*}
operators, removing the projection
Q
on the orthogonal complement of
\varphi_{0}
. The difference between
A
and

\widetilde{A}
has the form

A-\widetilde{A}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydz\ \left[\rho_{\sigma}(x;y;z)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{z}+\rho_{\gamma}(x;y;z)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{z}^{*}-h.c.\right].

(5.1)

with
\rho_{\sigma}=\nu_{\sigma}-(Q\otimes Q\otimes Q)\nu_{\sigma}
,
\rho_{\gamma}=\nu_{\gamma}-(Q\otimes Q\otimes Q)\nu_{\gamma}
and
\nu_{\sigma}(x,y,z)=\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y)\sigma_{L}(x,z)
,
\nu_{\gamma}(x,y,z)=\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y)\gamma_{L}(x,z)
. With the bounds

\begin{split}&\|\rho_{\gamma}\|,\|\rho_{\sigma}\|,\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\rho_{\gamma}(x,.,.)\|,\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\rho_{\gamma}(.,x,.)\|,\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\rho_{\gamma}(.,.,x)\|\leq C,\\
&\sup_{x,z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\rho_{\sigma}(x,.,z)\|,\sup_{y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\|\rho_{\sigma}(.,y,z)\|\leq C.\end{split}

(5.2)

it is possible to control terms arising from the difference (
5.1
) (estimating these terms on
\mathcal{F}^{\leq N}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}
is simple, because we consider states where all particles are orthogonal to
\varphi_{0}
). We will omit most details for these contributions.

5.1
Analysis of
e^{-A}[\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}-\mathcal{K}-\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}]e^{A}

We start by conjugating the quadratic part of
\mathcal{G}_{N}
, defined in (
2.31
), after subtracting the kinetic energy and the external potential (which will be considered in the next subsections). We will make use of the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
F:\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}
, with
F(y,x)=\bar{F}(x,y)
. Then there exists
C>0
such that

\pm\int F(x,y)\left[b_{x}^{*}b_{y},A\right]dxdy\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\min\left\{\|F\|_{2},\sup_{x}\int dy|F(x,y)|\right\}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}.

(5.3)

Moreover,

\pm\int F(x,y)\left[b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}+b_{y}b_{x},A\right]dxdy\leq\frac{C\|F\|_{2}}{\sqrt{N}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}.

(5.4)

Proof.

A tedious but straightforward computation shows that, on
\mathcal{F}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
,

\begin{split}\int dxdy\,&F(x,y)[b_{x}^{*}b_{y},A]\\
=\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)b(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})(1-\mathcal{N}/N)b(\overline{F}_{x})\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)b_{x}(1-\mathcal{N}/N)b(\overline{(F\widetilde{k}_{H})}_{x})\\
&-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\,b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})(1-\mathcal{N}/N)b(\overline{(\gamma_{L}F)}_{x})\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\,(1-\mathcal{N}/N)b_{x}b(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})b(\overline{(\sigma_{L}F)}_{x})\\
&-\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxdv\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\big(a^{*}(F_{v})a_{x}b(\overline{\widetilde{k}_{H,x}})+b_{x}a^{*}(F_{v})a(\overline{\widetilde{k}_{H,x}})\big)b_{v}\\
&+\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxdvb_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})a^{*}(F_{v})a(\overline{\gamma_{L,x}})b_{v}\\
&-\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxdv\,a^{*}(F_{v})a(\sigma_{L,x})b_{x}b(\overline{\widetilde{k}_{H,x}})b_{v}\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\ F(x,y)[b_{x}^{*}b_{y},A-\widetilde{A}]\\
=:\;&\sum_{j=1}^{7}\Upsilon_{j}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\ F(x,y)[b_{x}^{*}b_{y},A-\widetilde{A}].\end{split}

(5.5)

Here we interpret
F(x,y)
as the kernel of a symmetric operator, denoted again with
F
, and we use for example the notation
F\widetilde{k}_{H}
to indicate the product of the two operators
F
and
\widetilde{k}_{H}
. We can bound

\begin{split}|\langle\xi,\Upsilon_{1}\xi\rangle|\leq\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\,\|b(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\|\|b(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})b(\overline{F}_{x})\xi\|\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|\|b(\sigma_{L,x})b(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})b(\overline{F}_{x})(\mathcal{N}+1)^{-3/4}\xi\|\\
\leq\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left[\sup_{x}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|\right]\left[\|\gamma_{L}\|_{\text{op}}+\|\sigma_{L}\|_{\text{HS}}\right]\|F\|_{\text{op}}\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|^{2}.\end{split}

(5.6)

By Lemma
2.7
and because
\|F\|_{\text{op}}\leq\sup_{x}\int dy|F(x,y)|
we conclude that
\Upsilon_{1}\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}
. In (
5.6
), we used the fact that

\int dx\,\|b(F_{x})\xi\|^{2}\leq\langle\xi,d\Gamma(F^{2})\xi\rangle\leq\|F\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi\|^{2}

(and similarly with
F_{x}
replaced by
\gamma_{L,x}
). As for the second term on the r.h.s. of (
5.5
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\langle\xi,\Upsilon_{2}\,\xi\rangle|\leq\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\,\left[\|b(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\|+\|\sigma_{L,x}\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|\right]\|(F\widetilde{k}_{H})_{x}\|\|b_{x}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|.\end{split}

We can bound

\begin{split}\|(F\widetilde{k}_{H})_{x}\|_{2}^{2}=&\;\int\left|\int dyF(z,y)\widetilde{k}_{H}(y,x)dy\right|^{2}dz\\
=&\;\int F(z,y_{1})\widetilde{k}_{H}(y_{1},x)F(y_{2},z)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y_{2})dy_{1}dy_{2}dz\\
\leq\;&\int|F(z,y_{1})||F(y_{2},z)||\widetilde{k}_{H}(y_{1},x)|^{2}dy_{1}dy_{2}dz\leq\left[\sup_{z}\int|F(y,z)|dy\right]^{2}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

Alternatively, we can estimate
\|(F\widetilde{k}_{H})_{x}\|\leq\|F\|_{2}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|
. Thus, with Lemma
2.7
,

|\langle\xi,\Upsilon_{2}\xi\rangle|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\min\left\{\|F\|_{2},\sup_{x}\int dy|F(x,y)|\right\}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|^{2}.

The contribution of
\Upsilon_{3}
and
\Upsilon_{4}
can be bounded similarly as the one of
\Upsilon_{1}
(using the fact that
\|\gamma_{L}F\|_{\text{op}}\leq\|\gamma_{L}\|_{\text{op}}\|F\|_{\text{op}}\leq C\sup_{x}\int dy\,|F(x,y)|
and, analogously,
\|\sigma_{L}F\|_{\text{op}}\leq C\sup_{x}\int dy\,|F(x,y)|
). Let us consider
\Upsilon_{5}
. We first bring the term into normal order, then we have, with Lemma
2.7
,

\begin{split}|\langle\xi,\Upsilon_{5}\xi\rangle|&\leq\frac{2}{N^{3/2}}\int\|a(F_{v})(b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\xi\|\|a_{x}b(\overline{\widetilde{k}_{H,x}})b_{v}\xi\|dxdv\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dx\ \|(b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\xi\|\|b(\overline{\widetilde{k}_{H,x}})b(F_{x})\xi\|\\
&\leq\frac{3}{N^{3/2}}\|F\|_{\text{op}}\|\gamma_{L}\|_{\text{op}}\left[\sup_{x}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|\right]\|\mathcal{N}\xi\|\|\mathcal{N}^{3/2}\xi\|\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\left[\sup_{x}\int dy\,|F(x,y)|\right]\,\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|^{2}.\end{split}

The terms
\Upsilon_{6},\Upsilon_{7}
can be bounded similarly. As for
\Upsilon_{8}
, we proceed as explained after (
5.1
), using the bounds from (
5.2
). This completes the proof of (
5.3
).

To show (
5.4
), we compute the commutator

\int dxdy\,F(x,y)\left[b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}+b_{x}b_{y},A\right].

(5.7)

We find contributions similar to those appearing on the r.h.s. of (
5.5
), with some creation operators replaced by annihilation operators or vice versa. After normal ordering, these contributions can be bounded as
we did above. Through normal ordering, however, commutators produce new terms. Let us consider an example. Similarly to
\Upsilon_{1}
in (
5.5
), the operator (
5.7
) contains the term

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\,\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)b(\overline{\widetilde{k}_{H,x}})(1-\mathcal{N}/N)b^{*}(F_{x}).

Normal ordering produces a new term having the form (we focus here on the largest contribution to the commutator
[b(\overline{\widetilde{k}_{H,x}}),b^{*}(F_{x})]
)

\widetilde{\Upsilon}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\,\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\langle\overline{\widetilde{k}_{H,x}},F_{x}\rangle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\,\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)(F\widetilde{k}_{H})(x;x).

This contribution can be bounded, using Lemma
2.7
, by

\begin{split}|\langle\xi,\widetilde{\Upsilon}\xi\rangle|&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dx\|b(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\|\|\xi\||(F\widetilde{k}_{H})(x;x)|\leq\frac{C\|F\|_{2}}{\sqrt{N}}\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|\xi\|.\end{split}

(This term and similar contributions are the reason why (
5.4
) requires
F
to be an Hilbert-Schmidt operator). Other contributions emerging from (
5.7
) can be bounded analogously (or as we did above for the terms
\Upsilon_{j}
,
j=1,\dots,7
). Again, the contribution arising from
A-\widetilde{A}
can be controlled with the bounds in (
5.2
).
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}
denote the quadratic part of the excitation Hamiltonian
\mathcal{G}_{N}
, defined in (
2.31
).
Then, we have

e^{-A}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}-\mathcal{K}-\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}\big]e^{A}=\big[\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}-\mathcal{K}-\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}\big]+\mathcal{E}_{1}

where

\pm\mathcal{E}_{1}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}.

Proof.

We have

\begin{split}e^{-A}\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}e^{A}=\;&\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\int_{0}^{1}ds\,e^{-sA}\big[\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}},A\big]e^{sA}\\
=\;&\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\int_{0}^{1}ds\,\int dxdy\,\Phi^{\prime}(x;y)\,e^{-sA}\big[b_{x}^{*}b_{y},A\big]e^{sA}\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}ds\,\int dxdy\,\Gamma(x;y)\,e^{sA}\big[b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}+b_{x}b_{y},A\big]e^{sA}\end{split}

(5.8)

with
\Phi^{\prime}=\Phi-\left[-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}}\right]
and
\Phi
and
\Gamma
as defined in (
2.29
), (
2.30
). Let us consider first the diagonal term, proportional to
\Phi^{\prime}
. The contribution from terms in (
2.29
) that do not contain
-\Delta
or
V_{\text{ext}}
can be handled with (
5.3
) (using, on the r.h.s., the quantity
\sup_{x}\int dy\,|F(x,y)|
). To deal with the remaining terms (after subtracting
-\Delta
and
V_{\text{ext}}
) we observe that, by Lemma
2.7
, the operators

(\gamma-1)(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})\gamma,\quad(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})(\gamma-1),\quad\sigma(-\Delta+V_{\text{ext}})\sigma

all have a uniformly bounded Hilbert-Schmidt norm; thus we can apply again (
5.3
), this time using
\|F\|_{2}
on the right hand side. We conclude that

\begin{split}\left|\int dxdy\,\Phi^{\prime}(x;y)\,\langle\xi,e^{-sA}\big[b_{x}^{*}b_{y},A\big]e^{sA}\xi\rangle\right|&\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}e^{-sA}\xi\|^{2}\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|.\end{split}

where in the last step we used Lemma
5.1
.

Let us now consider the off-diagonal term on the r.h.s. of (
5.8
), proportional to
\Gamma
. Here, we use (
5.4
), combined with the bound (
3.6
) and the definitions (
2.30
), (
2.50
) showing that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{\text{HS}}\leq\|\widetilde{\Gamma}\|_{\text{HS}}+\|\sigma[N^{3}V(N.)*\varphi_{0}^{2}-8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\varphi_{0}^{2}]\gamma\|_{\text{HS}}\leq\|\widetilde{\Gamma}\|_{\text{HS}}+C\|\sigma\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C\,.

We obtain

\begin{split}\left|\int dxdy\,\Gamma(x;y)\,\langle\xi,e^{-sA}\big[b_{x}^{*}b^{*}_{y}+b_{x}b_{y},A\big]e^{sA}\xi\rangle\right|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|^{2}.\end{split}

Inserting in (
5.8
) and integrating over
s\in[0;1]
, we obtain the claim.
∎

5.2
Analysis
e^{-A}\mathcal{V}_{ext}e^{A}

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
0<6\tau\leq\varepsilon
, then we have

\pm\big[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},A\big]\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\left[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\right].

(5.9)

Proof.

The same tedious computation leading to (
5.5
) gives

\begin{split}\big[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},A\big]=\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,\big(V_{\text{ext}}(x)+V_{\text{ext}}(y)\big)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)b_{y}(1-\mathcal{N}/N)b_{x}\\
&-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdzV_{\text{ext}}(z)\gamma_{L}(x,z)b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})(1-\mathcal{N}/N)b_{z}\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdzV_{\text{ext}}(z)\sigma_{L}(x,z)b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})(1-\mathcal{N}/N)b^{*}_{z}\\
&-\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxduV_{\text{ext}}(u)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)a^{*}_{u}a_{x}b(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})a_{u}\\
&-\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxduV_{\text{ext}}(u)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)b_{x}a^{*}_{u}a(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})a_{u}\\
&+\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxduV_{\text{ext}}(u)b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})a^{*}_{u}a(\gamma_{L,x})b_{u}\\
&-\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxduV_{\text{ext}}(u)a_{u}^{*}a(\sigma_{L,x})a_{x}b(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})b_{u}+\text{h.c.}\\
&+[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},A-\widetilde{A}]\\
=:&\sum_{j=1}^{7}\Delta_{j}+\text{h.c.}+[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},A-\widetilde{A}].\end{split}

To bound the contribution proportional to
V_{\text{ext}}(y)
in
\Delta_{1}
, we use
\sup_{y}V_{\text{ext}}(y)\|\widetilde{k}_{H}(.,y)\|\leq C\sup_{y}V_{\text{ext}}(y)\varphi_{0}(y)\leq C
and we proceed similarly as in the bound (
5.6
). As for the part of
\Delta_{1}
proportional to
V_{\text{ext}}(x)
, we decompose
\gamma_{L,x}(y)=\check{g}_{L}(x-y)+(\gamma-1)*_{2}\check{g}_{L}(x;y)
. Terms arising from
(\gamma-1)*_{2}\check{g}_{L}
and also from
\sigma_{L,x}
can be handled as before, using the factor
\varphi_{0}(x)
to bound
V_{\text{ext}}(x)
. Thus, we only need to estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int dxdydz\ V_{\text{ext}}(x)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y)\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\langle\xi,b_{z}^{*}b_{y}b_{x}\xi\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\int dxdz\ V_{\text{ext}}(x)\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\|a_{x}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|\|a_{z}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\int dx\,V_{\text{ext}}(x)\|a_{x}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\left[\int dz\,(V_{\text{ext}}*\check{g}_{L})(z)\|a_{z}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\langle\xi,\big[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\big]\xi\rangle

using
(V_{\text{ext}}*\check{g}_{L})\leq C(V_{\text{ext}}+1)
. We conclude that

\pm(\Delta_{1}+\text{h.c.})\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\left[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\right].

To control
\Delta_{2}
, we set
\gamma^{\prime}_{L,x}=(\gamma-1)_{x}*\check{g}_{L}
. Since
\|V_{\text{ext}}\gamma^{\prime}_{L}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C
by Lemma
2.7
, we only need to estimate

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int V_{\text{ext}}(z)\check{g}_{L}&(x-z)\langle\xi,b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})b_{z}\xi\rangle\Big|\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\int V_{\text{ext}}(z)\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\|a_{x}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|\|a_{z}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|\\
&\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+1)^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|^{2}\end{split}

where, in the last step, we used the bound (
2.38
). We conclude that

\pm\Delta_{2}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\left[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\right].

The term
\Delta_{3}
can be handled similarly (here, there is no need to subtract the identity as we did with
\gamma_{L,x}^{\prime}
). As for
\Delta_{4}
, we use the inequalities
\sup_{x}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|_{2}\leq C
and
\sup_{x}\|\sigma_{L,x}\|_{2}\leq C
from Lemma
2.7
to get

\begin{split}|\langle\xi,\Delta_{4}\xi\rangle|\leq\;&\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxdu\,V_{\text{ext}}(u)\|b(\gamma_{L,x})a_{u}\xi\|\|a_{x}a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\\
&+\frac{1}{N^{3/2}}\int dxdu\,V_{\text{ext}}(u)\|a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|a_{x}a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\\
\leq\;&\frac{C}{N^{3/2}}\|\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/4}\xi\|^{2}.\end{split}

The terms
\Delta_{5},\Delta_{6},\Delta_{7}
can be bounded similarly. The contribution arising from the difference
A-\widetilde{A}
can be controlled proceeding as explained after (
5.1
), with (
5.2
).
∎

In order to use the estimate in Lemma
5.4
, we need to show that the r.h.s. of (
5.9
) remains small, when conjugated with
e^{A}
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
0<6\tau\leq\varepsilon
, then we have

e^{-A}\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}e^{A}\leq C\left[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}\right].

(5.10)

Proof.

To show (
5.10
) we compute, for an arbitrary
\xi\in\mathcal{F}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
,

\begin{split}\frac{d}{ds}\langle\xi&,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)e^{sA}\xi\rangle\\
=\;&\langle\xi,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)\big[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},A\big](\mathcal{N}+1)e^{sA}\xi\rangle+2\text{Re }\langle\xi,e^{-sA}\big[\mathcal{N},A\big]\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)e^{sA}\xi\rangle.\end{split}

(5.11)

The first term on the r.h.s. can be bounded with (
5.9
). We find

\langle\xi,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)\big[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},A\big](\mathcal{N}+1)e^{sA}\xi\rangle\leq C\langle\xi,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)\big(\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+\mathcal{N}+1\big)(\mathcal{N}+1)e^{sA}\xi\rangle.

As for the other terms, we compute

\begin{split}\langle e^{-sA}\xi,&\big[\mathcal{N},A\big]\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)e^{sA}\xi\rangle\\
=\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdu\,V_{\text{ext}}(u)\langle\xi,b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})\big(b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big)a_{u}^{*}a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdu\,V_{\text{ext}}(u)\langle\xi,\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)b_{x}b(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})a_{u}^{*}a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle.\end{split}

(5.12)

With
\sup_{x}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|<C
and
\|\sigma_{L}\|_{\text{HS}}\leq C
, we can control

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdu\,&V_{\text{ext}}(u)\langle\xi,b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big)a_{u}^{*}a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle\Big|\leq C\|\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|^{2}.\end{split}

The contribution from the first term on the r.h.s. of (
5.12
) proportional to
b(\gamma_{L,x})
, when arranged in normal order, produces a term of the form (we focus here on the largest term):

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdu\,V_{\text{ext}}(u)\,\gamma_{L}(x;u)\langle\xi,b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle.

(5.13)

Since
\gamma^{\prime}_{L}=(\gamma-1)*\check{g}_{L}
is such that

\sup_{x}\int du\,(V_{\text{ext}}(x)+V_{\text{ext}}(u))|\gamma^{\prime}_{L}(x;u)|\leq C,

it is enough to bound

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdu\,&V_{\text{ext}}(u)\,\check{g}_{L}(x-u)\langle\xi,b_{x}^{*}b^{*}(\widetilde{k}_{H,x})a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\rangle\Big|\\
\leq\;&\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdu\,V_{\text{ext}}(u)\,\check{g}_{L}(x-u)\|b_{x}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|a_{u}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|\\
\leq\;&\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+1)^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|^{2}\end{split}

where we used (
2.38
). As for the second term on the r.h.s. of (
5.12
), we have to move
a_{u}^{*}
to the left. This produces commutator terms, which can be handled similarly as we did with (
5.13
). The claim now follows inserting these bounds in (
5.11
), using Lemma
5.1
and Gronwall’s lemma.
∎

Combining the results of the last two lemmas, we can control the action of
A
on the external potential.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
0<6\tau\leq\varepsilon
, then we have

e^{-A}\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}e^{A}=\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+\mathcal{E}_{2}

where

\pm\mathcal{E}_{2}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\big[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3}\big].

Proof.

We can write

\mathcal{E}_{2}=\int_{0}^{1}dse^{-sA}\left[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}},A\right]e^{sA}.

From Lemma
5.4
, we find

\pm\mathcal{E}_{2}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\int_{0}^{1}dse^{-sA}\left[\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\right]e^{sA}.

Estimating
\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\leq\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}
, applying Lemma
5.1
, Lemma
5.5
and integrating over
s\in[0;1]
, we obtain the claim.
∎

5.3
Analysis of
e^{-A}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})e^{A}

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
0<6\tau\leq\varepsilon\leq 1/2
, then we have

\pm\big[\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N},A\big]\leq C\left(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\right).

(5.14)

Moreover, we can decompose

\big[\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N},A\big]=\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*}+\mathcal{E}

where

\Theta_{0}=-\sqrt{N}\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]

(5.15)

and where

\pm\mathcal{E}\leq CN^{-1/2+\varepsilon}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1).

(5.16)

Proof.

With a long but straightforward computation, we find

\begin{split}\big[\mathcal{K},A\big]=\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,\nabla_{x}\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y)\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\;\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\nabla_{x}\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{L,x})\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,\nabla_{y}\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y)\,b_{x}^{*}\nabla_{y}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydu\;\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\nabla\varphi_{0}(u)\varphi_{0}(x)\nabla_{u}a_{u}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydu\;\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\nabla\varphi_{0}(u)\varphi_{0}(y)a_{x}^{*}\nabla_{u}b_{u}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydz\;\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\Delta\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\Delta\sigma_{L,x})\big]+\text{h.c.}\\
&+\big[\mathcal{K},A-\widetilde{A}\big].\end{split}

(5.17)

Let us consider first the contribution proportional to
b(\nabla_{x}\gamma_{L,x})
in the second term on the r.h.s.
We write
\gamma_{L,x}(z)=\check{g}_{L}(x-z)+\gamma^{\prime}_{L,x}(z)
. Using that
\sup_{x}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|\leq C
, we find

\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\langle\xi,\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\nabla_{z}b_{z}\xi\rangle\Big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\xi\|.

(5.18)

The contribution proportional to
\gamma^{\prime}_{L}
can be estimated by

\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\langle\xi,\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b(\nabla_{x}\gamma^{\prime}_{L,x})\xi\rangle\Big|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi\|,

because
\|\nabla_{1}\gamma^{\prime}_{L}\|\leq C
by (
2.43
). As for the part of the second term on the r.h.s. of (
5.17
) proportional to
\nabla_{x}\sigma_{L,x}
we use that, by Lemma
2.7
,
\sup_{x}\|\nabla_{x}\sigma_{L,x}\|\leq CN^{\tau/2}
. Hence

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\;\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\langle\xi,&\nabla_{x}b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b^{*}(\nabla_{x}\sigma_{L,x})\xi\rangle\Big|\\
&\leq CN^{-1/2}\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|.\end{split}

To control the fourth and fifth term on the r.h.s. of (
5.17
), we proceed similarly.

As for the sixth term on the r.h.s. of (
5.17
), we write again
\gamma_{L,x}(z)=\check{g}_{L}(x-z)+\gamma^{\prime}_{L,x}(z)
. The contribution proportional to
\check{g}_{L}
can be decomposed after integration by parts into a term of the form (
5.18
) and the term

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}&\int dxdydz\,\nabla_{x}\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\langle\xi,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\nabla_{z}b_{z}\xi\rangle\Big|\\
\leq\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydz\,|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\|b_{x}\nabla_{y}b_{y}\xi\|\|\nabla_{z}b_{z}\xi\|\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydz\,|(Nw_{\ell}(N.)*\widecheck{\chi}_{H})(x-y)\nabla\varphi_{0}(y)|\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\|b_{x}b_{y}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{-1/2}\xi\|\|\nabla_{z}b_{z}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\\
\leq\;&\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\|(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N})^{1/2}\xi\|\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\end{split}

(5.19)

where we used
\nabla_{x}\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y)=-\nabla_{y}\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y)-(Nw_{\ell}(N.)*\widecheck{\chi}_{H})(x-y)\nabla\varphi_{0}(y)
and integration by parts. The contribution arising from the difference
A-\widetilde{A}
can be controlled with the strategy outlined after (
5.1
), with the bounds (
5.2
). We obtain

\displaystyle\pm[\mathcal{K},A-\widetilde{A}]\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\mathcal{N}^{3/2}.

We are left with the first and the third terms on the r.h.s. of (
5.17
). Here, we replace
\widetilde{k}_{H}
with

\widetilde{k}(x;y)=-Nw_{\ell}(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)
, using that, by , by (
2.41
),
\sup_{x}\|\nabla_{x}(\widetilde{k}-\widetilde{k}_{H})_{x}\|\leq CN^{\varepsilon}
. Integrating by parts, we conclude that

\begin{split}&\big[\mathcal{K},A\big]\\
&=\sqrt{N}\int dxdy\big[(\Delta_{x}+\Delta_{y})w_{\ell}(N(x-y))\big]\varphi_{0}(y)\,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{H,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{H,})\big]+\text{h.c.}+\mathcal{E}_{1}\\
&=\frac{2}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,N^{3}(\Delta w_{\ell})(N(x-y))\,\varphi_{0}(y)\,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{H,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{H,})\big]+\text{h.c.}+\mathcal{E}_{1}\end{split}

(5.20)

where

\displaystyle|\langle\xi,\mathcal{E}_{1}\xi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{\varepsilon-1/2}\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|+CN^{-1/2}\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N})^{1/2}\xi\|

\displaystyle\quad+CN^{-1/2}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|^{2}.

Here, we used the fact that contributions where one (or two) derivative hits
\varphi_{0}
produce error terms that can be bounded similarly as in (
5.19
).

We consider next the commutator with the potential energy operator
\mathcal{V}_{N}
. We find

\begin{split}\big[\mathcal{V}_{N},A\big]=\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydzdu\,N^{2}\big[V(N(x-u))+V(N(y-u))-V(N(z-u))\big]\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt\times\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\gamma_{L}(x;z)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}a_{u}^{*}b_{z}a_{u}\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydzdu\,N^{2}\big[V(N(x-u))+V(N(y-u))-V(N(z-u))\big]\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt\times\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\sigma_{L}(x;z)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}a_{u}^{*}b^{*}_{z}a_{u}\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydz\,N^{2}\big[V(N(x-z))+V(N(y-z)\big]\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\sigma_{L}(x;z)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{z}^{*}\\
&+\text{h.c.}+[\mathcal{V}_{N},A-\tilde{A}].\end{split}

(5.21)

To control the second term on the r.h.s. of (
5.21
), we write
\gamma_{L}(x;z)=\check{g}(x-z)+\gamma^{\prime}_{L}(x;z)
. Focusing for example on the contribution proportional to
V(N(x-u))
and to
\check{g}_{L}
, we can proceed as follows:

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}&\int dxdydzdu\,N^{2}V(N(x-u))\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\langle\xi,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}a_{u}^{*}b_{z}a_{u}\xi\rangle\Big|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydudz\,N^{2}V(N(x-u))|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|\,\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\,\|b_{x}b_{y}a_{u}\xi\|\|b_{z}a_{u}\xi\|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\Big[\int dxdydudz\,N^{2}V(N(x-u))\,\check{g}^{2}_{L}(x-z)\,\|b_{x}b_{y}a_{u}\xi\|^{2}\Big]^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\times\Big[\int dxdydudz\,N^{2}V(N(x-u))|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|^{2}\,\|b_{z}a_{u}\xi\|^{2}\Big]^{1/2}\\
&\leq CN^{(3\tau-\varepsilon)/2-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|\mathcal{N}\xi\|\leq CN^{-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|\mathcal{N}\xi\|\end{split}

where we used the fact that
\sup_{x}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|\leq CN^{-\varepsilon/2}
,
\|\check{g}_{L}\|\leq CN^{3\tau/2}
shown in Lemma
2.7
. The other contributions to the second term on the r.h.s. of (
5.21
) can be treated similarly. Also the third term on the r.h.s. of (
5.21
) can be treated analogously. As for the fourth term on the r.h.s. of (
5.21
), we can proceed as follows (focussing for example on the contribution proportional to
V(N(x-z))
):

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydz\,&N^{2}V(N(x-z))\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\sigma_{L}(x;z)\langle\xi,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b_{z}^{*}\xi\rangle\Big|\\
\leq\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdydz\,N^{2}V(N(x-z))|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)||\sigma_{L}(x;z)|\\
&\hskip 142.26378pt\times\|b_{x}b_{y}b_{z}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{-1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\\
\leq\;&N^{(3\tau-\varepsilon)/2-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\leq CN^{-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|.\end{split}

The contribution arising from the difference
A-\widetilde{A}
can again be controlled using (
5.2
). We find

\displaystyle|\langle\xi,[\mathcal{V}_{N},A-\widetilde{A}]\xi\rangle|\leq CN^{-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|.

We are left with the first term on the r.h.s. of (
5.21
). Here we replace
\widetilde{k}_{H}
with

\widetilde{k}(x;y)=-Nw_{\ell}(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)
, using that, by (
2.41
),
|(\widetilde{k}-\widetilde{k}_{H})(x;y)|\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{0}(y)
. We conclude that

\begin{split}&\big[\mathcal{V}_{N},A\big]\\
&=-\sqrt{N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))w_{\ell}(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]+\text{h.c.}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\end{split}

where

|\langle\xi,\mathcal{E}_{2}\xi\rangle|\leq CN^{-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|+CN^{\varepsilon-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|.

(5.22)

Combining (
5.20
) with (
5.22
) and using the scattering equation (
2.6
), we obtain

\big[\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N},A\big]=-\sqrt{N}\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]+\text{h.c.}+\mathcal{E}_{3}

where the error term
\mathcal{E}_{3}
takes into account the error terms
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}
and also the contributions emerging from the r.h.s. of (
2.6
). From (
5.20
), (
5.22
), we obtain that

\displaystyle|\langle\xi,\mathcal{E}_{3}\xi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq CN^{\varepsilon-1/2}\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|+CN^{-1/2}\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N})^{1/2}\xi\|

\displaystyle\quad+CN^{-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|+CN^{\varepsilon-1}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|

\displaystyle\quad+CN^{-1/2}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/4}\xi\|^{2}.

Hence, using
\varepsilon\leq 1/2
, we obtain as well

\pm\mathcal{E}_{3}\leq C\left[\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{K}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\right].

To conclude the proof of the lemma, we just have to observe that

\pm\sqrt{N}\left(\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\,b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big]+h.c.\right)\leq C(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1).

∎

As an application of (
5.14
), we establish a bound on the growth of
\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N}
with respect to the action of
A
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
0<6\tau\leq\varepsilon\leq 1/2
. For
k\in\{0,2\}
, there is a constant
C>0
such that, for all
s\in[0;1]

e^{-sA}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k}e^{sA}\leq C(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k+2}.

(5.23)

Proof.

For a fixed
\xi\in\mathcal{F}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
and for
s\in[0;1]
, let

\varphi_{\xi}(s)=\langle\xi,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k}e^{sA}\xi\rangle=\langle\xi,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k/2}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k/2}e^{sA}\xi\rangle.

We compute

\begin{split}\frac{d}{ds}\varphi_{\xi}(s)=\;&\langle\xi,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k/2}\big[\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N},A\big](\mathcal{N}+1)^{k/2}e^{sA}\xi\rangle\\
&+\Big\{\langle\xi,e^{-sA}\big[(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k/2},A\big](\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k/2}e^{sA}\xi\rangle\xi\rangle+\text{hc.}\Big\}.\end{split}

(5.24)

With Lemma
5.7
, the term on the first line is bounded by

\begin{split}\langle\xi,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k/2}\big[\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N},A\big](\mathcal{N}+1)^{k/2}e^{sA}\xi\rangle&\leq\varphi_{\xi}(s)+\langle\xi,e^{-sA}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k+2}e^{sA}\xi\rangle\\
&\leq\varphi_{\xi}(s)+C\langle\xi,(\mathcal{N}+1)^{k+2}\xi\rangle\end{split}

where in the last step we applied Lemma
5.1
. If
k=0
, (
5.23
) follows by Gronwall’s Lemma. If
k=2
, we still have to handle the terms on the second line of (
5.24
). To this end, we observe that, writing
\widetilde{A}=A_{\gamma}+A_{\sigma}-A^{*}_{\gamma}-A^{*}_{\sigma}
, with

A_{\gamma}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b(\gamma_{L,x}),\qquad A_{\sigma}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dxdy\,\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x}),

we find, as
\mathcal{N}
preserves
\mathcal{F}_{\perp\varphi_{0}}^{\leq N}
,

\big[\mathcal{N},A\big]=\big[\mathcal{N},\widetilde{A}\big]=A_{\gamma}+3A_{\sigma}+3A_{\sigma}^{*}+A_{\gamma}^{*}

and thus

\big[\mathcal{N},A\big](\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})=\left\{A_{\gamma}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})+3A_{\sigma}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})+\text{hc.}\right\}+3\big[A_{\sigma}^{*},(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})\big]+\big[A_{\gamma}^{*},(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})].

To control these terms, we can proceed similarly as in the proof of Lemma
5.7
. We skip the details.
∎

Since the term
\Theta_{0}
defined in (
5.15
), emerging from the commutator of
\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N}
with
A
, is not small, we need to conjugate it again with
e^{A}
. To this end, we will use the following lemma.

Lemma 5.9
.

We assume
0<3\tau\leq\varepsilon
. Let
\Theta_{0}
be defined as in (
5.15
). Let

\begin{split}\Pi_{0}=\;&-\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\big(b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\\
&-\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\big(b(\gamma_{L,y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,y})\big).\end{split}

(5.25)

Then we have

\big[\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*},A\big]=\Pi_{0}+\Pi_{0}^{*}+\mathcal{E}

(5.26)

where

\pm\mathcal{E}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\big[\mathcal{V}_{N}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\big].

Moreover,

\pm\big[\Pi_{0}+\Pi_{0}^{*},A\big]\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}.

(5.27)

Proof.

We compute

\begin{split}\big[\Theta_{0}&+\Theta_{0}^{*},A\big]\\
=\;&\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dudvN^{3}V(N(u-v))\varphi_{0}(v)\big[b_{u}^{*},A\big]b_{v}^{*}\big(b(\gamma_{L,u})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,u})\big)+\text{h.c.}\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dudvN^{3}V(N(u-v))\varphi_{0}(v)b_{u}^{*}\big[b_{v}^{*},A\big]\big(b(\gamma_{L,u})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,u})\big)+\text{h.c.}\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\int dudvN^{3}V(N(u-v))\varphi_{0}(v)b_{u}^{*}b_{v}^{*}\big[\big(b(\gamma_{L,u})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,u})\big),A\big]+\text{h.c.}\\
&+\big[\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*},A-\widetilde{A}\big]\\
=\;&\text{I}+\text{II}+\text{III}+\big[\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*},A-\widetilde{A}\big].\end{split}

(5.28)

Let us first consider the operator
I
. With the expression (
2.45
) for
A
, the commutator
[b_{u}^{*},A]
produces several terms.
From (
2.5
), we can also predict that corrections to the canonical commutation relations carry an additional
N^{-1}
factor and will therefore lead to small errors. An example of such a term contributing to
I
is

\begin{split}\Big|\frac{1}{N^{2}}\int&dudvdxdyN^{3}V(N(u-v))\varphi_{0}(v)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\langle\xi,b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})a_{u}^{*}b_{v}^{*}a_{x}b_{y}b(\gamma_{L,u})\xi\rangle\Big|\\
&\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\int dudvdxdyN^{3}V(N(u-v))\varphi_{0}(v)|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|\|a_{u}a_{v}a(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\|\|a_{x}a_{y}a(\gamma_{L,u})\xi\|\\
&\leq\frac{C}{N^{3/2}}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{1/2}\xi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{3/2}\xi\|.\end{split}

Other contributions of order 6 in creation and annihilation operators can be bounded similarly. Let us now focus on terms of order 4, which appear from the term
I
if we use canonical commutation relations for the
b
-fields. The main contribution arises from the part of
A
containing 2 or 3 annihilation operators. It has the form (from
[b_{u}^{*},b_{x}]\simeq\delta(u-x)
)

\begin{split}\int&dvdxdyN^{2}V(N(x-v))\varphi_{0}(v)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)b_{y}b_{v}^{*}\big(b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\\
\simeq\;&\int dvdxdyN^{2}V(N(x-v))\varphi_{0}(v)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\times\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)b_{v}^{*}b_{y}\big(b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\\
&+\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\big(b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x})\big)\end{split}

(5.29)

where again we used the fact that corrections to the canonical commutation relations produce only small errors.
The first term on the r.h.s. of the last equation is small, on states with few excitations. In fact, we can bound (considering for example the contribution proportional to
b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})
and to
b(\gamma_{L,x})
)

\begin{split}\Big|\int dv&\,dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-v))\varphi_{0}(v)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\langle\xi,b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})b_{v}^{*}b_{y}b(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\rangle\Big|\\
\leq\;&\int dvdxdydz\,N^{2}V(N(x-v))\varphi_{0}(v)|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\|a_{z}a_{v}\xi\|\|b_{y}b(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\|\\
&+\int dvdxdydz\,N^{2}V(N(x-v))\varphi_{0}(v)|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|\|a(\gamma^{\prime}_{L,x})a_{v}\xi\|\|b_{y}b(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\|\end{split}

(5.30)

where we used the notation
\gamma^{\prime}_{L}=(\gamma_{L}-1)*_{2}\hat{g}_{L}
. The first term on the r.h.s. of (
5.30
) can be estimated (using that
\sup_{x}\|\widetilde{k}_{H,x}\|\leq C<\infty
) by

\begin{split}\int dvdxdy&dz\,N^{2}V(N(x-v))\varphi_{0}(v)|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|\check{g}_{L}(x-z)\|a_{z}a_{v}\xi\|\|b_{y}b(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\|\\
&\leq\left[\int dvdxdydz\,N^{2}V(N(x-v))|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|^{2}\|a_{z}a_{v}\xi\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\times\left[\int dvdxdydz\,N^{2}V(N(x-v))\check{g}^{2}_{L}(x-z)\|a_{y}a(\gamma_{L,x})\xi\|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\\
&\leq CN^{-1+(3\tau-\varepsilon)/2}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\xi\|^{2}.\end{split}

The second term on the r.h.s. of (
5.30
) can be bounded using that
\sup_{x}\|\gamma^{\prime}_{L,x}\|<\infty
. There are many more contributions to
I
that are quartic in creation and annihilation operators (some of them appear in the first term on the r.h.s. of (
5.29
)); they can all be controlled analogously (in fact, the term (
5.29
) is the only one producing a non-negligible quadratic contribution; the reason is that the two commutators leading to the second term on the r.h.s. of (
5.29
) contract the variables in the kernel
\widetilde{k}_{H}
with the variables in the interaction potential). We conclude that

\text{I}=\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-v))\varphi_{0}(y)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)(b(\gamma_{L,x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,x}))+\mathcal{E}_{1}

where

\pm\mathcal{E}_{1}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\big[\mathcal{V}_{N}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\big].

The term
II
on the r.h.s. of (
5.28
) can be handled similarly. We find

\text{II}=\int dxdyN^{2}V(N(x-v))\varphi_{0}(x)\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\big)(b(\gamma_{L,y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{L,y}))+\mathcal{E}_{2}

with an error
\mathcal{E}_{2}
satisfying the same estimate as
\mathcal{E}_{1}
. To bound
III
we can proceed similarly. We obtain

\pm\text{III}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\big[\mathcal{V}_{N}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\big].

The contribution arising from
A-\widetilde{A}
can be handled as indicated in (
5.1
), with the bounds in (
5.2
). We obtain

\displaystyle|\langle\xi,[\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*},A-\widetilde{A}]\xi\rangle|\leq\frac{C}{N^{3/2}}\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\|\mathcal{N}^{2}\xi\|.

This proves (
5.26
).

To show (
5.27
), we can use Lemma
5.2
. To this end, we just need to observe that the kernel
s(x;y):=N^{2}V(N(x-y))(\varphi_{0}(y)+\varphi_{0}(x))|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)|
is such that
\sup_{x}\int dy|s(x;y)|<\infty
, uniformly in
N
. This follows readily from
|\widetilde{k}_{H}(x,y)|\leq CN\varphi_{0}(y).

∎

Combining Lemma
5.7
with Lemma
5.8
and Lemma
5.9
, we can now compute precisely the action of
A
on the operator
\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N}
.

Corollary 5.10
.

Let
0<6\tau\leq\varepsilon\leq 1/2
, then we have

e^{-A}(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N})e^{A}=\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N}+\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*}+\frac{1}{2}\left[\Pi_{0}+\Pi_{0}^{*}\right]+\mathcal{E}

where

\pm\mathcal{E}\leq CN^{\varepsilon-1/2}\left[(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{K})(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{4}\right].

5.4
Analysis of
e^{-A}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}e^{A}

Lemma 5.11
.

Let

\begin{split}\Xi_{0}=\;&\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)\,\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\left(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\right)\left(b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\right)\\
\;&+\int dxdy\,N^{2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\,\widetilde{k}_{H}(x;y)\left(b^{*}(\gamma_{L,x})+b(\sigma_{L,x})\right)\left(b(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y})\right).\end{split}

(5.31)

Then we have

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}},A\big]=\Xi_{0}+\Xi_{0}^{*}+\mathcal{E}

where

\pm\mathcal{E}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\big[\mathcal{V}_{N}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\big].

Moreover,

\displaystyle\pm\big[\Xi_{0}+\Xi_{0}^{*},A\big]\leq CN^{-1/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}.

Since the operator
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}
is very similar to
\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*}
, with
\Theta_{0}
as defined in (
5.16
), Lemma
5.11
can be shown very similarly to Lemma
5.9
. We skip the details.
With Lemma
5.11
(and using Lemma
5.8
) to bound the growth of
\mathcal{V}_{N}
), we can control
the action of
A
on
\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}
.

Lemma 5.12
.

We have

e^{-A}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}e^{A}=\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\Xi_{0}+\Xi_{0}^{*}+\mathcal{E}

where

\pm\mathcal{E}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\big[\mathcal{V}_{N}+(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\big].

5.5
Proof of Prop.
2.8

Combining (
2.33
) with Lemma
5.3
, Lemma
5.6
, Lemma
5.7
and Lemma
5.12
, we conclude that

\mathcal{J}_{N}=e^{-A}\mathcal{G}_{N}e^{A}=\kappa_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\mathcal{V}_{N}+\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*}+\Xi_{0}+\Xi_{0}^{*}+\frac{1}{2}\big[\Pi_{0}+\Pi_{0}^{*}\big]+\mathcal{E}

(5.32)

where

\pm\mathcal{E}\leq CN^{\varepsilon-1/2}\big[(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}+\mathcal{V}_{N}\big].

From (
2.32
) and (
5.15
), we find (with
\delta
denoting Dirac’s delta-distribution)

\begin{split}\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}&+\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*}\\
=\;&\int dxdy\,N^{5/2}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(y)b_{x}^{*}b_{y}^{*}\big[b(\gamma_{x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L}))+b^{*}(\sigma_{x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L}))\big]\\
&+\text{h.c.}\end{split}

(5.33)

Thus, we find

\begin{split}\Big|\langle\xi,&\big(\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*}\big)\xi\rangle\Big|\\
&\leq C\|\mathcal{V}_{N}^{1/2}\xi\|\,\Big\{\int dxdyN^{3}V(N(x-y))\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\times\big[\|b(\gamma_{x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L}))\xi\|^{2}+\|b^{*}(\sigma_{x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L}))\xi\|^{2}\big]\Big\}^{1/2}.\end{split}

(5.34)

Decomposing
\gamma=1+p_{\eta}
, we find

\|b(\gamma_{x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L}))\xi\|^{2}\leq\|b((\delta-\check{g}_{L})_{x})\xi\|^{2}+\|b(p_{\eta,x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L}))\xi\|^{2}.

Switching to Fourier space, we estimate

\int dx\|b((\delta-\check{g}_{L})_{x})\xi\|^{2}=\int dp\ (1-g_{L}(p))^{2}\|\hat{b}_{p}\xi\|^{2}\leq CN^{-2\tau}\|\mathcal{K}^{1/2}\xi\|^{2}.

On the other hand,

\|b(p_{\eta,x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L}))\xi\|^{2}\leq\|p_{\eta,x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L})\|^{2}\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\xi\|^{2}

(5.35)

and

\begin{split}\int dx\,\|p_{\eta,x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L})\|^{2}&=\int dxdy\,\Big|\int dz\,\big[p_{\eta}(x;y)-p(x;z)\big]\check{g}_{L}(y-z)\Big|^{2}\\
&\leq\int dxdydz\big[p_{\eta}(x;y)-p(x;z)\big]^{2}\check{g}_{L}(y-z)\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}ds\int dxdydz\big|(\nabla_{2}p)_{\eta}(x;sy+(1-s)z)\big|^{2}(y-z)^{2}\check{g}_{L}(y-z)\\
&\leq CN^{-2\gamma}\|\nabla_{2}p_{\eta}\|\end{split}

where, in the last step, we shifted the integration variable
y\to y+z
.
To bound the second term in the parenthesis on the r.h.s. of (
5.34
), we decompose
\sigma_{x}=k_{x}+(\sigma-k)_{x}
. The contribution proportional to
(\sigma-k)_{x}
can be controlled similarly as in (
5.35
). To control the contribution proportional to
k_{x}
, on the other hand, we switch to Fourier space. Using Lemma
2.1
and (
A.1
) we get
|\hat{k}_{x}(p)|\leq C|p|^{-2}\varphi_{0}(x)

and therefore

\displaystyle\int dx\ \|k_{x}*(\delta-\check{g}_{L})\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\int dxdp\ |\hat{k}_{x}(p)|^{2}(1-g_{L}(p))^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{|p|<N^{-\tau}}dp\ \frac{1}{|p|^{4}}N^{-2\tau}|p|^{2}+C\int_{|p|\geq N^{-\tau}}dp\ \frac{1}{|p|^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq CN^{-\tau}.

From (
5.34
), Lemma
5.1
and Lemma
5.8
, we conclude that

\pm\big[\mathcal{C}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\Theta_{0}+\Theta_{0}^{*}\big]\leq CN^{-\tau/2}(\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{N}+1).

Finally, we compare the terms
\Xi_{0},\Pi_{0}
on the r.h.s. of (
5.32
) with the operator

\begin{split}Z_{0}=\;&-\int dxdyN^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi^{2}_{0}(y)w_{\ell}(N(x-y))\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big)\big(b(\gamma_{x})+b^{*}(\sigma_{x})\big)\\
&-\int dxdyN^{3}V(N(x-y))\varphi_{0}(x)\varphi_{0}(y)w_{\ell}(N(x-y))\big(b^{*}(\gamma_{x})+b(\sigma_{x})\big)\big(b(\gamma_{y})+b^{*}(\sigma_{y})\big)\end{split}

where we removed all cutoffs (recall the definition (
5.25
) of
\Pi_{0}
and (
5.31
) of
\Xi_{0}
, with the kernels
\widetilde{k}_{H}
and
\gamma_{L},\sigma_{L}
introduced in (
2.37
), (
2.39
)). The restriction to small momenta inserted in the kernels
\gamma_{L},\sigma_{L}
can be removed
similarly as in (
5.33
) (here, the comparison is a bit easier, because we are dealing with quadratic, rather than cubic, expressions in creation and annihilation operators). To remove the restriction to high momenta in the kernel
\widetilde{k}_{H}
, we notice that,
we have
|(\widetilde{k}-\widetilde{k}_{H})(x,y)|\leq CN^{\varepsilon}\varphi_{0}(y)
by (
2.41
).
We conclude that

\pm\big[(\Xi_{0}+\Xi_{0}^{*})-(Z_{0}+Z_{0}^{*})\big]\leq CN^{-\tau/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)+CN^{-\tau}\mathcal{K}

and that

\pm\big[(\Pi_{0}+\Pi_{0}^{*})+(Z_{0}+Z_{0}^{*})\big]\leq CN^{-\tau/2}(\mathcal{N}+1)+CN^{-\tau}\mathcal{K}.

Thus

\mathcal{J}_{N}=\kappa_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\big[Z_{0}+Z_{0}^{*}\big]+\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{E}

where

\pm\mathcal{E}\leq CN^{\varepsilon-1/2}\big[(\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{\text{ext}}+1)(\mathcal{N}+1)^{2}\big]+CN^{-\tau/2}\big[\mathcal{K}+\mathcal{V}_{N}+\mathcal{N}+1\big].

The proposition follows by noticing that

\kappa_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{G}_{N}}+\frac{1}{2}\big[Z_{0}+Z_{0}^{*}\big]=\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}+\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}

with
\kappa_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{J}_{N}}
defined as in (
2.47
) and (
2.48
) and
\pm\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\leq CN^{-1}\mathcal{N}
being an error term arising from normal ordering.

Appendix A
Properties of the Gross-Pitaevskii Functional

In this appendix we collect several well-known results about the Gross-Pitaevskii functional
\mathcal{E}_{GP}
, defined in equation (
1.4
). Let us recall that
\mathcal{E}_{GP}:\mathcal{D}_{GP}\to\mathbb{R}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\varphi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(|\nabla\varphi_{0}(x)|^{2}+V_{\text{ext}}(x)|\varphi_{0}(x)|^{2}+4\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}|\varphi_{0}(x)|^{4}\right)dx

with domain

\mathcal{D}_{GP}=\big\{\varphi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{3}):\ V_{\text{ext}}|\varphi|^{2}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\big\}.

Recall, moreover, assumption
(2)
in Eq. (
1.7
) on the external potential
V_{\text{ext}}
. The following was proved in
[
20
, Theorems 2.1, 2.5 & Lemma A.6]
.

Lemma A.1
.

There exists a minimizer
\varphi_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{GP}
with
\|\varphi_{0}\|_{2}=1
such that

\inf_{\psi\in\mathcal{D}_{GP}\ :\|\psi\|_{2}=1}\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\psi)=\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\varphi_{0}).

The minimizer
\varphi_{0}
is unique up to a complex phase, which can be chosen so that
\varphi_{0}
is strictly positive. Furthermore, the minimizer
\varphi_{0}
solves the Gross-Pitaevskii equation

\displaystyle-\Delta\varphi_{0}+V_{\text{ext}}\varphi_{0}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}|\varphi_{0}|^{2}\varphi_{0}=\varepsilon_{GP}\varphi_{0},

with
\mu
given by

\varepsilon_{GP}=\mathcal{E}_{GP}(\varphi_{0})+4\pi\mathfrak{a}_{0}\|\varphi_{0}\|_{4}^{4}.

Moreover,
\varphi_{0}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and for every
\nu>0
there exists
C_{\nu}
(which only depends on
\nu
and
\mathfrak{a}_{0}
) such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
it holds true that

|\varphi_{0}(x)|\leq C_{\nu}e^{-\nu|x|}.

(A.1)

As in the main text,
\varphi_{0}
denotes the unique, strictly positive minimizer of
\mathcal{E}_{GP}
, subject to the contraint
\|\varphi_{0}\|_{2}=1
. In addition to Lemma
A.1
, we collect some additional facts about the regularity of
\varphi_{0}
. The following was shown in
[
11
, Theorem A.2]
.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
V_{\text{ext}}
satisfy the assumptions in (
1.7
). Then
\varphi_{0}\in H^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})\cap C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
and for every
\nu>0
there exists
C_{\nu}>0
such that for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
we have

|\nabla\varphi_{0}(x)|,\ |\Delta\varphi_{0}(x)|,|\nabla\Delta\varphi_{0}(x)|\leq C_{\nu}e^{-\nu|x|}.

(A.2)

Furthermore, if
\widehat{\varphi}_{0}
denotes the Fourier transform of
\varphi_{0}
, we have for all
p\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
that

|\widehat{\varphi}_{0}(p)|,|\widehat{\varphi_{0}^{2}}(p)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|p|)^{3}}.

(A.3)
